Alternative Action for Generalized Unimodular Gravity
Dmitry Nesterov

Abstract
We present an alternative formulation of generalized unimodular gravity (GUMG), a class of modifications to general relativity characterized by a special partial breaking of general coordinate covariance. The action for this formulation is derived constructively through a sequence of equivalent representations, starting from the original GUMG setup and extending the configuration space and gauge structure by introducing time parametrization. Our approach, based on a canonical formalism, parallels the method used by Henneaux and Teitelboim to covariantize the action of unimodular gravity (UMG), which was generalized to consistently accommodate a parameterization via local fields for the entire GUMG family. For completeness, we explore the dynamical structure of the theory and provide a detailed account of its gauge properties. A notable consequence of the consistent parametrization is the emergence of explicit spatial delocalization in the action, manifestations of which we carefully examine. Within the GUMG family, we identify a subfamily of models described by a local action. While preserving the essential structural features, these models avoid the complications of spatial nonlocality allowing a clearer analysis. The dynamical and constraint structures of GUMG family models are different from those of UMG, however the latter appears as an exceptional special case within the family, providing valuable comparative insights. .

Theory Department, P.N. Lebedev Physical Institute of the Russian Academy of Sciences, Leninsky Prospekt 53, Moscow, 119991, Russia

⋆
e-mail: nesterov@lpi.ru

Introduction

The exploration of gravity theories with broken general covariance dates back to Einstein, who examined such frameworks in 1919
[
2
]
. Thus Unimodular gravity (UMG) — a significant model that retains key features of general relativity (GR), emerged nearly at the dawn of modern gravitational physics and gained renewed interest in late eighties,
[
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
]
. A useful advantage of this theory is an alternative covariant formulation, proposed by Henneaux and Teitelboim,
[
8
]
. Over the past decade, the search for experimentally viable modifications of general relativity has been influenced by the Horava-Lifshitz models
[
9
,
10
,
11
]
, where spacetime covariance is explicitly broken to only spatial covariance, allowing to improve ultraviolet behavior.

Generalized unimodular gravity (GUMG)
[
12
,
13
,
14
,
15
]
in combines specific aspects of both approaches. Like UMG, it can be described by simply restricting the configuration space of general relativity. At the same time it breaks spacetime covariance in a substantial way, introducing a preferred time direction and a corresponding spatial slicing of spacetime. Remarkably, although spatial diffeomorphism invariance is also implicitly broken in GUMG, this violation does not generate explicit spatial anisotropy, allowing the theory to retain some of the benefits of spatial covariance within the formalism.

Generalized unimodular gravity models originally were introduced in
[
12
]

\displaystyle S[g,{\lambda}_{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}]=\int\!dt\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\sqrt{|g|}\,{{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}(g)-\int\!dt\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,{\lambda}_{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\big({N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}{-}F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\big)\,,

(1)

motivated by search for dark energy candidates. Here
g_{{\mu}{\nu}}
is an unrestricted metric of
\mathrm{d}
-dimensional spacetime, which can be decomposed into Arnowitt–Deser–Misner (ADM) metric components
[
16
,
17
]
as

g_{{\mu}{\nu}}d{X}^{{\mu}}d{X}^{{\nu}}=\gamma_{{m}{n}}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{m}{+}{{N}}^{m}dt)(d{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{n}{+}{{N}}^{n}dt)-{{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}}^{2}dt\hskip 1.0ptdt\,,

(2)

with lapse function
{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
, shift functions
{N}^{m}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
and the induced metric
\gamma_{{m}{n}}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
on
t{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}const
hypersurfaces. The first term in (
1
) is the standard Einstein-Hilbert action of general relativity and the second term implements the generalized unimodular restriction condition,

{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\eomeq F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\,,

(3)

which restricts the lapse function
{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
to be equal to some positive-definite function
F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){\,>\,}0

of the induced metric determinant,
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\defeq\sqrt{\det\gamma_{{m}{n}}}
. A choice of the characteristic function
F
fixes the particular theory. In the particular case
F{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}
, condition (
3
) reproduces the restriction condition of unimodular gravity, which justifies the name of the GUMG family and allows for certain checks to be carried out.

The observation, which attracted attention to GUMG models, is that the restriction term in the action (
1
) generates the energy-momentum tensor of the cosmological perfect fluid,

\displaystyle T^{{\mu}{\nu}}=\varrho\,u^{\mu}u^{\nu}+p\,(g^{{\mu}{\nu}}+u^{\mu}u^{\nu})\,,

(4)

on the right hand side of Einstein equations. In (
4
)
u^{\mu}
— timelike unit vector field, normal to
t=const
hypersurfaces. Perfect fluid’s energy is
\varrho\defeq\frac{{\lambda}_{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}}{2\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}
and pressure is
p\defeq\frac{{\lambda}_{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}}{2\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}\frac{FW}{{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}}
,
which on the restriction surface
{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\eomeq F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
implies the equation of state

\displaystyle p\eomeq W\varrho\,,

(5)

where the role of barotropic parameter is played by the derivative characteristic function
1
1

1

To avoid additional branching of the canonical constraint structure we assume
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
to be sign-definite, which implies the monotonicity of
F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
(these characteristic functions are defined on domain
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}{\,>\,}0
). However, it is not the strict condition. For example, the particular
dust
case
W{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
also fits the formalism.

\displaystyle W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\,\defeq\,\frac{{d}{\hskip 0.8pt}{\,\ln F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})}}{{d}{\hskip 0.8pt}{\,\ln{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}}}\,.

(6)

The dependence of
W
on the cosmological scale of spatial sections is an attractive feature, as the coefficient in the equation of state typically depends on the redshift parameter, according to phenomenology of the Universe evolution (for recent reviews see
[
18
,
19
]
2
2

2

Authors of
[
19
]
refer to homogeneous cosmological models, characterized by the dependance of
W
on the conformal factor of spatial sections, as
W
CDM models. They note, however, that such parametrization “has no physical motivation, but is commonly used as an ansatz in data analysis to quantify differences and distinguish between dark energy models”. GUMG models present a novel perspective on their potential physical origin.

).

Dynamical and constraint analysis of GUMG models disproved some original hopes, but uncovered various interesting features
[
14
]
. Notably, it was shown that GUMG
suggests new inflation mechanism consistent with observations within a certain subclass of
F

[
15
]
.

This work continues the general analysis of the generalized unimodular gravity theories. Here we construct and analyze an alternative representation for GUMG models
[
12
,
14
]
, analogous to the Henneaux–Teitelboim representation
[
8
]
for unimodular gravity. We discuss the dynamic and gauge structure of the resulting representation as well as related technical issues, including peculiarities of parametrization of the field theory models.

Formally, the key result is a constructive proof that the general GUMG model can be equivalently described by the action

\displaystyle S_{alt}[\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}]=\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}\;\big({{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g)-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\big)+\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\>\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral B\pdfliteral}\hskip 1.0pt\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\,,

(7)

where configuration-space variables are the components of the bulk auxiliary metric
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g_{{\mu}{\nu}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
, cosmological-constant field
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
and the auxiliary vector field
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{{\mu}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
.
The operator
\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral B\pdfliteral}({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
is a local in time but in general spatially nonlocal linear invertible operator of the form

\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral B\pdfliteral}=\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\,,

(8)

where the spatial delocalization is encoded in the invertible operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
,
the explicit definition of which is given in Section
4.2
and basic properties are discussed in the Appendix
A.1
. Here we just note that though the choice of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
is not entirely unique, there is a natural one —
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt(W)
, (
124
), functionally dependent on
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
only and expressible as a sum of two projectors. This choice is all the more convenient because for the subfamily of models with

W\equiv\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}=const\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad F\propto{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\vphantom{|^{l}}}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}\,,\qquad[\text{$\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}$-GUMG}]

(9)

operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt(W)
becomes the identity operator, which makes the action and its equations of motion manifestly local. We will henceforth refer to the subfamily (
9
) as
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG and use it to highlight key features of the gauge structure and dynamics of (
7
) without technical complications associated with spatial nonlocality.
In the particular case of unimodular gravity, corresponding to

W\equiv-1\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad F\propto{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\vphantom{|^{|}}}^{-1}\,,\qquad[\text{UMG}]

(10)

the entire operator
\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral B\pdfliteral}({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
, (
8
), becomes the identity operator, which reduce action (
7
) to the Henneaux–Teitelboim covariant UMG action
[
8
]
.

We present a detailed analysis of dynamical properties of the theory (
7
). In Section
4.3
we also discuss the consequences of spatial delocalization. In particular, the effect may be seen in the on-shell behaviour of the cosmological-constant field
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
(
136
), which feels the global properties of
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
,

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\,\propto\,\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}F^{-1}\frac{W^{-1}}{\,\overline{W^{-1}}\,\vphantom{I^{|^{I}}}}\,,\vskip-5.69054pt

(11)

where
\overline{W^{-1}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\overline{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
is the average of the
W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
over the
\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}const\,
spatial hypersurface.

The key step linking the original and alternative representations of GUMG models is the consistent implementation of time reparametrization, which we carry out in the canonical formalism. While conceptually this is similar to the procedure of Henneaux and Teitelboim for unimodular gravity
[
8
]
, a direct generalization is applicable only for the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG subfamily of models. The naive transfer of the mechanical parametrization
[
23
]
to a field theory generally does not provide an equivalent theory with local constraints.
At the same time, due to the specific constraint structure of GUMG models,
a functionally complete modified parametrization constraint can still be recovered. This may be achieved by a mechanical-like parameterization using local in time but spatially global fields, then by introducing a purely gauge sector of average-free auxiliary fields, and finally making a linear transformation of constraints.
This approach localizes the canonical phase space, potentially keeping simple spatial nonlocality in the constraint structure encoded by the operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
.
We provide a detailed discussion of the consistent parametrization analyzing the emergence and nature of this spatial nonlocality.

We carefully examine the gauge structure of the resulting action and show that it also exhibits spatial nonlocality. In particular, it manifests in the fact that time reparametrization acts homogeneously on the metric sector. However, the spatial nonlocality of the gauge structure arises for reasons only indirectly related to those that generate
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
: the nonlocality persists even in manifestly local
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG models, reflecting the mixed-class functional nature of constraints. In an appropriate constraint basis, only the spatially homogeneous component of parametrization constraint is first-class, whereas the complementary average-free component is second-class. Among spatial diffeomorphisms, only those with divergence-free parameters are first-class. In GUMG models with nonconstant
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
, the representation of gauge transformations becomes more involved due to the spatial nonlocality in the action. However, the gauge structure across all nonexceptional GUMG theories remains qualitatively similar.

The article is organized as follows.

In Section
2
, we briefly review the canonical formulation of generalized unimodular gravity and complete it with comments on exceptional cases. This section establishes the canonical extended action and constraint structure as the starting point for parameterization.

In Section
3
, we focus on the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG subfamily (
9
), deriving its Henneaux–Teitelboim-like action and analyzing its dynamical properties and gauge structure. We take the opportunity to highlight various GUMG-specific dynamical features, and to examine the gauge-structure spatial nonlocality for a manifestly local model, free from the nonlocalities introduced by
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
.

In Section
4
, we extend the analysis to general GUMG models, with particular emphasis on the spatial delocalization associated with
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
and the consistent parameterization.

The Appendices provide additional technical and conceptual details.
In Appendix
A
we summarize the properties of the delocalization operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
and related operators.
In Appendix
B
we take out some calculational details concerning gauge structures.
In Appendix
C
we present the Lagrangian form of the theory when parameterized only by spatially-homogeneous fields.
In Appendix
D
we discuss the applicability of the developed technique and results to the case of manifolds with noncompact spatial sections.
In Appendix
E
we collected together canonical structure relations for
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG and general GUMG theories.

2
Canonical Form of Generalized Unimodular Gravity

In this section, we discuss the canonical formalism for GUMG theory (
1
) and its constraint structure. This largely follows the observations of
[
14
]
, but is rewritten here using concise hamiltonization, where canonically conjugate momenta are introduced only for the induced metric on constant-time hypersurfaces
\gamma_{{m}{n}}
, the only dynamical component of the spacetime metric
g_{{\nu}{\mu}}
(
2
).

The GUMG action (
1
), reduced with respect to fields
{\lambda}_{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
and
{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
, which form a pair of auxiliary variables
3
3

3

By
auxiliary variables
we mean a subset of variables that can be expressed algebraically in terms of other fields by solving a subsystem of variational equations with respect to only these variables. The full action and the action reduced by eliminating the auxiliary variables are physically equivalent.
, up to total derivatives reads

\displaystyle S_{red}[\gamma,{N}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\int\!dt\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\big(K^{m}_{\,{n}}K^{n}_{\,{m}}-K^{m}_{\,{m}}K^{n}_{\,{n}}+R(\gamma)\big)\,.

(12)

The configuration space of the reduced theory (
12
) consists of the induced metric
\gamma_{{m}{n}}
and shift functions
{N}^{n}
, (
2
).
R(\gamma)
denotes the scalar curvature of the induced metric on constant-time sections and the ‘‘restricted’’
extrinsic curvature is

\displaystyle K_{{m}{n}}=\frac{1}{2F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})}\big(\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}-{N}_{{m};{n}}-{N}_{{n};{m}}\big)\,.

(13)

Spatial indices, denoted by Latin letters, are raised and contracted by the inverse spatial metric
\gamma^{{m}{n}}
. A
dot
denotes for time derivative, a
semicolon
indicates spatial covariant derivatives, while a
colon
represents partial derivatives with respect to the spatial coordinate.

2.1
Canonical extended action

Concise transition to the canonical theory requires conjugated momenta only for
\gamma_{{m}{n}}
,

\displaystyle\pi^{{m}{n}}

\displaystyle\!=\!

\displaystyle\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}\mathcal{L}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}}\>=\>\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\big(K^{{m}{n}}-\gamma^{{m}{n}}K^{{k}}_{\,{k}}\big)\,.

(14)

After the Legendre transform, the action (
12
) acquires
primary
canonical form

\displaystyle S_{{\scriptscriptstyle P}}[\gamma,\pi,{N}]=\int\!dt\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}-F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}(\gamma,\pi)-{N}^{n}{H}_{n}(\gamma,\pi)\big)\,.

(15)

The phase-space structures
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}(\gamma,\pi)
and
{H}_{n}(\gamma,\pi)
are identical to the Hamiltonian and momentum constraints in canonical Einstein gravity
[
17
]
,

\displaystyle{{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}(\gamma,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}\Big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\pi_{{m}{n}}-\frac{1}{\mathrm{d}{-}2}\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt^{2}\Big)-\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\,R(\gamma)\,,

(16)

\displaystyle{{H}}_{n}(\gamma,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\,\gamma_{{n}{m}}{\pi^{{m}{k}}}_{\!;{k}}\,,

(17)

where
\pi_{{m}{n}}\defeq\gamma_{{m}{k}}\pi^{{k}{l}}\gamma_{{l}{n}}
and
\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt\defeq\gamma_{{m}{n}}\pi^{{m}{n}}
. The presence of constraint structures from the parental gauge theory is generically expected in restricted theories
4
4

4

We refer to Einstein gravity as
parental
to GUMG in the sense of
[
20
]
as the gauge theory from which the considered
restricted
model is obtained by imposing algebraic restriction, which fixes some gauge and/or non-gauge degrees of freedom.
, though their roles may change. In (
15
), the second structure (
17
) is still defines constraint

{H}_{n}(\gamma,\pi)=0\,,

(18)

entering the canonical action with Lagrange multipliers
{N}^{m}
, while the first structure (
16

F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}(\gamma,\pi)
.
In the concise setup, the phase-space variables are (
\gamma_{{m}{n}},\pi^{{m}{n}}
), and the Poisson bracket of functions
A(\gamma,\pi)
,
B(\gamma,\pi)
is given by

{\mathchoice{\big\{{A},{B}\big\}}{\big\{{A},{B}\big\}}{\{{A},{B}\}}{\{A,B\}}}={\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(\frac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}}{A}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}{\gamma_{{m}{n}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}}\frac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}}{B}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}{\pi^{{m}{n}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}}-\frac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}}{A}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}{\pi^{{m}{n}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}}\frac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}}{B}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt}{\gamma_{{m}{n}}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}}\big)\,,
where
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}...
denotes the
spatial
integration over the
t=const
hypersurface,
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}...\defeq\int\limits_{t=const}\!\!\!d{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,...\,
.

The Dirac consistency procedure
[
21
,
22
,
23
]
, which in its first step requires the preservation of primary constraints
{H}_{m}=0
in time,

\dot{{{H}}}_{n}\;\eomeq\;\big(WF{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}\big)_{,{n}}\!+{{N}^{m}}_{,{n}}{H}_{m}+\big({N}^{m}{H}_{n}\big)_{,{m}}\,\weq\,0\;,

(19)

reveals secondary constraints

\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}(\gamma,\pi)\;=\;0\,,

(20)

where we introduce the phase-space function

\mathcal{T}(\gamma,\pi)\;\equiv\;W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}(\gamma,\pi)\,.

(21)

This is where the equation-of-state parameter of the cosmological perfect fluid

W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})=\tfrac{d\ln{F}}{d\ln\!{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}}\,,
(
6
), is explicitly incorporated into the canonical formalism. If
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
for certain values of
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}
, it would induce constraint branching at this step. Though it does not affect the main result, to avoid further complication of the constraint structure we assume sign-definiteness of
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
and thus monotonicity of
F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
.

The new constraint (
20
) takes the form of a gradient, where the derivative is the partial spatial derivative. Since
\mathcal{T}
is not a scalar function
5
5

5

{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}(\gamma,\pi)
is a scalar density of weight
1
(like
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}
). Structures
F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
,
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
generically do not have a definite weight, unless they are homogeneous in
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}
.

under spatial diffeomorphisms, this suggests a partial breaking of spatial covariance. The latter is confirmed by the analysis of the constraint structure in Section
2.2
, which shows that the longitudinal part of the spatial diffeomorphisms is a second-class constraint.

Notably, the constraint (
20
) is equivalent to
functionally incomplete
scalar constraint
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}=0
,

\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}=0\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}=0\,,

(22)

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
denotes the spatially average-free part of
\mathcal{T}
. Any local function
f(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
is uniquely decomposed into average (homogeneous) and average-free (inhomogeneous) components

f(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})=\overline{f}(t)+\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717ptf\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\;:\qquad\begin{array}[]{|l}\;\overline{f}(t)\defeq\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\,,\vphantom{\big|^{l}}\quad\;{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}\defeq{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}1\,,\\
\;\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717ptf\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\defeq f(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})-\overline{f}(t)\,,\\
\end{array}\qquad

(23)

where integrals
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}
denote spatial integration and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}
is the background (coordinate) volume of the
t{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}const
hypersurface
6
6

6

In GUMG models, the background spatial volume is gauge-invariant on the branch where the decomposition (
23
) applies. This is because only
transverse
diffeomorphisms (
36
), associated with unit-Jacobian spatial coordinate transformations, remain as gauge symmetries, thereby preserving spatial integrals with a fixed measure.

.
The two subspaces are orthogonal, which implies
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f\,h\,{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}\overline{f}\,\overline{h}{\,+}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\widetilde{f}\>\widetilde{h}
and
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\overline{f}\,\widetilde{h}\,{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\overline{f}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\widetilde{h}\,{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\,0
.
While the explicit form of definitions on the right of (
23
) is straightforward for compact spatial sections, a similar projection decomposition is applicable for noncompact spatial sections as well (see Appendix
D
).

Preservation of the secondary constraint
\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}\weq 0
in time leads to the condition

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{T}}_{,{n}}(\gamma,\pi)\;\eomeq\;\partial_{n}\big(\mathcal{T}\,\mathcal{S}\big)+\partial_{n}\big(W\partial_{m}(F^{2}\gamma^{{m}{k}}{H}_{k})\big)+\partial_{n}\big({N}^{m}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}\big)\,\weq\,0\,,

(24)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\gamma,\pi,{N})\;\defeq\;\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\,(\partial{N})-\Theta({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\,\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt\;,\vphantom{\Big|_{\big|}}

(25)

\displaystyle\displaystyle{\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\defeq\frac{{d}{\hskip 0.8pt}{\ln{W}}}{{d}{\hskip 0.8pt}{\ln\!\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}}+W+1\;,\qquad\Theta({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\defeq\frac{1}{\mathrm{d}{-}2}\,\frac{{d}{\hskip 0.8pt}{\ln{W}}}{{d}{\hskip 0.8pt}{\ln\!{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}}}\,\frac{F}{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}}\,.

(26)

The two latter terms in (
24
) vanish on constraint surface
{H}_{n}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
,
\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
. So, given
(\mathcal{T}\hskip 1.0pt\mathcal{S})_{,{n}}=\mathcal{T}\hskip 1.0pt\mathcal{S}_{,{n}}{+\,}\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}\mathcal{S}
, the consistency condition (
24
) generates the constraint

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\,\mathcal{S}_{,{n}}=0\,.

(27)

Importantly,
\mathcal{T}
does not vanish on the constraint surface — only its average-free part,
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
, does.

At this point a peculiar feature of generalized unimodular gravity emerges. The consistency condition for the secondary constraint takes a factorizable form, signaling a bifurcation of the constraint set into two dynamical branches,

\begin{array}[]{lll|lll}&\text{\emph{Non-GR branch}}&&&\quad\text{\emph{GR branch}}&\\
\hline\cr&{H}_{n}=0\,,&&&{H}_{n}=0\,,&\\
&\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}=0\,,\;\;(\mathcal{T}\neq 0)&&&\mathcal{T}=0\;\;(\Leftrightarrow\,{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}=0)\,.&\\
\end{array}

(28)

The constraint set of the second branch coincides with the constraint set of the parental theory — Einstein’s general relativity.
For this reason, we refer to the second branch as the
GR branch
and the first as
non-GR branch
.

Within the concise hamiltonization approach, the Dirac consistency procedure ends at this step, as preservation in time of the constraint
\mathcal{T}\,\mathcal{S}_{,{n}}=0
does not introduce new restrictions on the constraint surface. But the underlying mechanisms differ between the two branches.

The GR branch (
{H}_{n}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
,
\mathcal{T}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
) is preserved in time since

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{T}}(\gamma,\pi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\,\mathcal{S}+W\partial_{n}(F^{2}\gamma^{{n}{m}}{H}_{m})+{N}^{n}\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}\overset{\footnotesize{{H}_{n},\mathcal{T}}}{\approx}0\,.

(29)

The preservation is predictable, because the constraints are equivalent to the constraint set of the parental gauge theory with closed algebra (
{H}_{n}{=\,}0
,
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
), and the Hamiltonian
F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
on this branch weakly vanishes.

In contrast, the condition
\mathcal{S}_{,{n}}{=\,}0
, (
25
), of the non-GR branch (
{H}_{n}{=\,}0
,
\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}{=\,}0
,
\mathcal{T}\neq 0
)
does not impose additional constraints on the phase space since it restricts the divergence of the Lagrange multipliers
7
7

7

The solution (
30
) implicitly assumes that the derivative characteristic function
\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
, (
26
), should be sign-definite. The assumption
\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\neq 0
imposes a convexity inequality on the characteristic function
F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
and prevents further branching of the canonical constraints structure. As discussed in Section
2.3
, GUMG models from the exceptional subfamily
\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){\,\equiv\>}0
are dynamically too restrictive, if not to say pathological, except important regular UMG case (
10
), for which
\Theta{\,\equiv\>}0
and
\mathcal{S}
does not appear.

\displaystyle(\partial{N})\,\eomeq\,\widetilde{U}_{0}(\gamma,\pi)\,\defeq\,\Omega^{-1}\Theta\,\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt-\Omega^{-1}\frac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\Omega^{-1}\Theta\,\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\Omega^{-1}}\,.

(30)

For
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG subfamily
W{\,\equiv\>}\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}const
, (
9
), the average-free structure
\widetilde{U}_{0}(\gamma,\pi)
vanishes, as
\Omega=\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{+}1
and
\Theta\propto\frac{{d}{\hskip 0.8pt}{W}}{{d}{\hskip 0.8pt}{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}}\equiv 0
. In the general GUMG case,
\widetilde{U}_{0}(\gamma,\pi)
remains nontrivial.

Finally, the
extended
canonical action of the GUMG theory may be written as

\displaystyle S_{{\scriptscriptstyle E}}[\gamma,\pi,{N},\mu]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\int\!dt\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}-F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}-{N}^{n}{H}_{n}-\mu^{n}\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}\big)\,.

(31)

While it looks as the extended action for the non-GR branch, it describes the dynamics on both branches, explicitly incorporating their total constraint surface.

2.2
Constraint structure

A review of canonical GUMG would be incomplete without mentioning the constraint structure. In the basis
{H}_{m}
,
\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}
, the Poisson brackets of the constraints are

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{m}{H}_{m}}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{m}{H}_{m}}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{m}{H}_{m}}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n},{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{m}{H}_{m}\}}}&{\!=\!}&{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\mathchoice{{\big[{\xi},{\eta}\big]^{{n}}}}{{\big[{\xi},{\eta}\big]^{{n}}}}{{[{\xi},{\eta}]^{{n}}}}{{[{\xi},{\eta}]^{{n}}}}}{H}_{n}\;\;\weq\;\;0\,,\\
{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n},{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}\}}}&{\!=\!}&{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}(\partial\eta)\,\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}+{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\partial\xi)(\partial\eta)\,\Omega\,\mathcal{T}\;\;\weq\;\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\partial\xi)(\partial\eta)\,\Omega\,\overline{\mathcal{T}}\,,\\
{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{{n}}\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{{n}}\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{{n}}\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{{n}}\mathcal{T}_{,{n}},{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}\}}}&{\!=\!}&{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big((\partial\xi)\hskip 1.0pt\accentset{\leftrightarrow}{\partial}\hskip-1.0pt_{{n}}{(\partial\eta)}\big)F^{2}W^{2}\gamma^{{n}{m}}{H}_{m}\;\;\weq\;\;0\,,\\
\end{array}

where
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
and
\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
are defined in (
6
) and (
26
), and the vectors
\xi^{n}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
,
\eta^{m}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
are arbitrary local functions. We use the notation
\,(\partial\xi)\defeq\xi^{{n}}_{\,,{n}}\,
for the vector divergence,

(f\hskip 1.0pt\accentset{\leftrightarrow}{\partial}\hskip-1.0pt_{{n}}g)\defeq(fg_{,{n}}{-}f_{,{n}}g)
for the antisymmetric derivative, and
{\mathchoice{{\big[{\xi},{\eta}\big]^{{n}}}}{{\big[{\xi},{\eta}\big]^{{n}}}}{{[{\xi},{\eta}]^{{n}}}}{{[{\xi},{\eta}]^{{n}}}}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{\xi}^{m}\partial_{m}{\eta}^{n}{-}{\eta}^{m}\partial_{m}{\xi}^{n}
for the Lie bracket.

The right-hand side, revealing nontrivial contributions on the complete constraint surface (
{H}_{n}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
,
\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
), demonstrates that the rank of the Poisson bracket matrix is
nonconstant
. On the
non-GR branch
(
{H}_{m}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
,
\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
,
\mathcal{T}{\,\neq\>}0
), the constraints
\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}
and the longitudinal part of
{H}_{m}
form a second-class pair, while the
transverse
8
8

8

We refer to the diffeomorphisms (
36
) with divergence-free gauge parameters as
transverse
.
It is worth clarifying that this decomposition is covariant with respect to a background metric of unit determinant, rather than with respect to the physical metric
\gamma_{{m}{n}}
.

momentum constraint,

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{H}_{n}\,,\qquad(\partial\xi_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}})\equiv{\xi_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{\,{n}}}\vphantom{|\xi}_{,{n}}=0\,,

(36)

is first-class.
This follows from the fact that form factors of
{H}_{n}
appear on the right side of (
2.2
) only through divergence (form factors at
\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}
always form divergence combination due to the constraint’s gradient form).
On the
GR branch
(
{H}_{n}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
,
\mathcal{T}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
), the rank of the constraint matrix (
2.2
) is zero implying that all constraints are first-class.
This offers an alternative viewpoint on the irregular nature of the GUMG constraint structure, as having a Poisson bracket matrix with non-constant rank.

Transverse spatial diffeomorphisms form a gauge subalgebra in the full algebra of spatial diffeomorphisms

{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{m}{H}_{m}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{H}_{n}}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{m}{H}_{m}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{H}_{n}}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{m}{H}_{m}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{H}_{n}}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{m}{H}_{m},{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{H}_{n}\}}}{\;=\;}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\lambda}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{m}{H}_{m},
where
{\lambda}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{\mathchoice{{\big[{{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}},{{\eta}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}\big]^{{n}}}}{{\big[{{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}},{{\eta}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}\big]^{{n}}}}{{[{{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}},{{\eta}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}]^{{n}}}}{{[{{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}},{{\eta}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}]^{{n}}}}}
, and
(\partial\lambda_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}){\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
for
(\partial\xi_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}){\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
and
(\partial\eta_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}){\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
.
The transverse components of a vector field,
{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\xi^{m}{P_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}_{m}^{\,{n}}
, can be extracted using the projector
{P_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}_{n}^{\,{m}}\defeq\delta_{n}^{\,{m}}-\partial_{n}\tfrac{1}{\vartriangle_{0}}\partial_{k}\gamma_{0}^{{k}{m}}
, where
\frac{1}{\vartriangle_{0}}
is the inverse of the symmetric second-order spatial differential operator
\vartriangle_{0}\!\defeq\partial_{m}\gamma_{0}^{{m}{n}}\partial_{n}
, constructed using an auxiliary nondegenerate symmetric form
9
9

9

If the operator
\vartriangle_{0}
is degenerate, as for compact manifolds, its inverse is defined to be symmetric, with its kernels coinciding with those of
\vartriangle_{0}
(the Moore-Penrose inverse
[
25
]
).
Note that
\vartriangle_{0}\!\!{\,\defeq\>}\partial_{m}\gamma_{0}^{{m}{n}}\partial_{n}
is covariant under coordinate transformations with the unit Jacobian, corresponding to the gauge freedom of the model.

\gamma_{0}^{{m}{n}}
.
Transverse diffeomorphisms

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{H}_{n}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\xi^{n}{P_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}_{n}^{\,{m}}{H}_{m}
correspond to spatial coordinate transformations with a unit determinant of the Jacobian.
The second-class longitudinal part of
{H}_{n}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
, can be expressed as

\tfrac{1}{\vartriangle_{0}}\partial_{m}(\gamma_{0}^{{m}{n}}{H}_{n}){\;=\;}0\,.
Consequently, the constraint term in the action can be decomposed into the first- and second-class contributions

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{N}^{n}{H}_{n}\,{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{N}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{H}_{n}{\,-\,}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\partial{N})\tfrac{1}{\vartriangle_{0}}\partial_{m}(\gamma_{0}^{{m}{n}}{H}_{n})\,,
where
{N}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{n}{\,\defeq\>}{P_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}_{n}^{\,{m}}{N}^{m}
and
(\partial{N}){\,\defeq\>}{N}^{{n}}_{\,,{n}}
.
The parametric dependence of such longitudinal-transverse decomposition on
\gamma_{0}^{{m}{n}}
does not compromise consistency.
Change of
\gamma_{0}^{{m}{n}}
induces a shift by a transverse combination,

\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt_{\!\gamma_{0}}{P_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}_{n}^{\,{m}}=-\partial_{n}\tfrac{1}{\vartriangle_{0}}\partial_{k}(\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\gamma_{0}^{{k}{l}}){P_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}_{k}^{\,{m}}
,
which preserves divergence-free property of the transverse vectors. Likewise,

\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt_{\!\gamma_{0}}\tfrac{1}{\vartriangle_{0}}\partial_{l}\gamma_{0}^{{l}{n}}{H}_{n}=\tfrac{1}{\vartriangle_{0}}\partial_{l}(\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt\gamma_{0}^{{l}{k}}){P_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}}_{k}^{\,{n}}{H}_{n}
,
which indicates that the ambiguity in the longitudinal second-class component stems from arbitrary shifts by first-class transverse diffeomorphisms.

The presence of second-class constraints on the non-GR branch imposes on-shell conditions on the corresponding Lagrange multipliers
[
23
]
. The system of consistency equations for constraints yields a particular solution

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{|llllll}\;{N}^{n}&\!\eomeq&U_{0}^{n}\;\defeq\;\gamma_{0}^{{n}{m}}\partial_{m}\frac{1}{\vartriangle_{0}}\Big(\Omega^{-1}\Theta\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt-\Omega^{-1}\frac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\Omega^{-1}\Theta\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\Omega^{-1}}\Big)\,,\\
\;{\mu}^{n}&\!\eomeq&0\,,\end{array}

where
{U_{0}^{\,{n}}}\vphantom{|\xi}_{,{n}}
coincides with
\widetilde{U}_{0}
from (
30
). Since the consistency conditions constrain only divergences
(\partial{N})
and
(\partial\mu)
, the general solution is obtained by adding arbitrary transverse vectors on the right-hand sides of (
2.2
).

As noted in
[
14
]
, the GR and non-GR branches (
28
) describe different numbers of degrees of freedom. While the functional dimensions of the configuration space and constraint surface are the same, the difference lies in the number of first-class constraints and the corresponding gauge-equivalence classes. The GR branch has
{\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}{-}3)}/{2}
local physical degrees of freedom, which is one less than
{(\mathrm{d}{-}1)(\mathrm{d}{-}2)}/{2}
on the non-GR branch.

2.3
Exceptional cases

To complete the discussion of the standard formulation of GUMG, we note important exceptional cases within the family (
12
) based to its canonical description.

Unimodular case
W{\,\equiv\>}{-}1
.

This is the case of
unimodular
gravity corresponding to
F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\propto\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}
, (
10
).
Its exceptional status in the family arises from
\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){\,\equiv\>}0
. Relations (
2.2
) imply that the rank of the Poisson brackets matrix of constraints is regular and equals zero. Thus, all constraints (
{H}_{m}
,
\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}
) are first-class and there is no division of the physical space into two dynamical branches.

This theory describes
\mathrm{d}(\mathrm{d}{-}3)/2
local gravitational degrees of freedom, similar to GR.

Other cases with
\Omega{\,\equiv\>}0
.

There are non-unimodular cases where
\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){\,\equiv\>}0
.
These cases correspond to restriction functions

F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\propto|\,b{\,+}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}|
with some overall positive constant and another arbitrary constant
b\neq 0
.
Due to
\Omega=0
for
W\neq const
the consistency procedure starting from the secondary constraint (
24
) generate a chain of further constraints:

\widetilde{\frac{\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt}{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}}
, etc. , making these cases exceptional and potentially pathological.

Dust case
W{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
.

This case corresponds to a pressureless perfect fluid with

F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\propto 1\,.
In is notable because, unlike generic GUMG, it possesses a regular canonical constraint structure. The regularity occurs because the consistency condition of the primary constraints (
19
) does not generate secondary constraints
\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}
(
20
).
All primary constraints
{H}_{m}
are first-class, exhibiting an unbroken spatial-diffeomorphism gauge invariance.

However, the extended action (
31
) also describes the dust case. The manually added constraint
\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}\to\alpha{{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}_{,{m}}
, with some constant
\alpha
(instead of
WF)
, serves as a partial gauge-fixing condition for the longitudinal part of
{H}_{n}
.
This describes
{(\mathrm{d}{-}1)(\mathrm{d}{-}2)}/{2}
local degrees of freedom, as in a general GUMG theory.

As we have already mentioned, theories in which
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
or
\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
vanish at certain values of
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}
exhibit additional branching and can therefore also be considered exceptional.

3
Alternative Action for
W=const
GUMG Theories

In this section we derive and discuss an alternative Henneaux–Teitelboim-like form of the action for the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG subfamily defined by (
9
)

W\,\equiv\,\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}=const\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}\propto\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}\,,

(40)

keeping in mind the exceptional status of the cases
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{-}1
and
0
. The constancy of
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
implies that the derivative functions (
26
) also take the simple forms

\displaystyle\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\,\equiv\,\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}+1\,,\;\quad\Theta({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\,\equiv\,0\,.

(41)

Following the reasoning that led Henneaux and Teitelboim to derive the so-called covariant action for unimodular gravity
[
8
]
, we obtain an alternative action that extends their approach to
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG models. The locality of this procedure within this subfamily makes it a valuable intermediate step toward analyzing the general GUMG case.

3.1
Parameterized canonical action

The first step is to parameterize the extended action (
31
) restricted to
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG,

\displaystyle S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{{\scriptscriptstyle E}}[\gamma,\pi,{N},\mu]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\int\!dt\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}-F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}-{N}^{n}{H}_{n}-\mu^{n}\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{}_{,{n}}\big)\,,

(42)

where
F\mapsto F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}\defeq\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}
and
\mathcal{T}\mapsto\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}\defeq\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
.
The secondary constraint structure
\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}
contains
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
, the Hamiltonian constraint of general relativity. However, the constraint
\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{}_{,{n}}{=\,}0
is functionally incomplete, as it lacks a spatially homogeneous mode.
Parameterizing the action with the Hamiltonian
F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
should naturally restore this missing component.

Motivated by
[
8
]
, in this section we adopt a naive, local parametrization prescription,

\displaystyle S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}loc}_{par}[\gamma,\pi,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{\prime}]\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!+\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}\!)-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{n}{H}_{n}\!-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{\prime{n}}\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{}_{,{n}}\big)\,,\qquad

(43)

which introduces time parametrization into the canonical action
by adding an auxiliary canonical field pair,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
, and converting the Hamiltonian (shifted by
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
) into a new constraint with the Lagrange multiplier
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
.
While this prescription is always valid in mechanics, it generally fails in field theory, making it necessary to verify physical equivalence.

The extended theory (
42
), with the constraint set
\big({H}_{n},\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{}_{,{n}}\big)
, and the parameterized theory (
43
), with the enlarged constraint set
\big((\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}),{H}_{n},\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{}_{,{n}}\big)
, are physically equivalent if the following two conditions are satisfied. First, the rank of the Poisson bracket matrices for both sets must be preserved, meaning the enlarged constraint space introduces a new
first-class
constraint. This constraint may be either the newly added one or a linear combination of the new and original constraints, provided the coefficient of the new constraint is full-rank.
Second, an admissible (partial) gauge fixing must exist such that, when imposed on the parameterized action (
43
), it recovers the original extended action (
42
) upon reduction.

From the Poisson-bracket relations

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}),{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})\}}}\;=\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(f{g}_{,{n}}\!{-}{g}{f}_{,{n}}\big)F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{2}\gamma^{{n}{m}}{H}_{m}\;\weq\;0\,,\\
{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{n}{H}_{n}}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{n}{H}_{n}}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{n}{H}_{n}}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}),{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\eta}^{n}{H}_{n}\}}}\;=\;(\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{+}1){\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\partial\eta)\,F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip The general solution involves

The existence of such first-class constraint ensures that the rank of the Poisson bracket matrix of constraints is preserved while parameterizing.

On the right-hand side of (
48
), only the homogeneous part of the Hamiltonian,
F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
, survives. This is how spatial nonlocality arises in the gauge structure of the local
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG parameterized theory (this issue is further addressed in Section
3.4
). The new first-class parametrization constraint generates spatially local gauge transformations in the auxiliary variables sector and nonlocal (spatially homogeneous) transformations of the metric fields.

The gauge-fixing condition that transforms​ (
43
)​ into​ (
42
),
called the​
