,​ is

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t=0\,.

(49)

The new first-class constraint (
48
) is transversal to the gauge-fixing condition (
49
), which supports the admissibility of this gauge. It is clear how the reduction of the partially gauge-fixed parameterized system with respect to the second-class constraints
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}{-}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t
leads to (
42
): the reduction eliminates these two constraints while restoring the Hamiltonian
F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
on the reduction surface from the term
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
(where
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\to-F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
,
\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\to 1
). This competes the check of the physical equivalence between the parameterized and original theories.

The success of the
local
implementation of time parametrization in
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG, (
43
),
stems from the fact that the average-free part of the local Hamiltonian,
F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
, is constrained to zero by the secondary constraint (
22
), which is equivalent to
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\widetilde{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
. This is nontrivial, as an analogous local parametrization is generally not possible in field theory.

To emphasize the role of the constraint (
22
) in ensuring the locality of the parameterized action (
43
), consider an alternative stepwise approach to parameterization, which provides a foundation for extending the procedure to general GUMG models. Starting from the extended action (
42
), one can always introduce a set of canonically conjugate spatially homogeneous fields,
\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
and
\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
, along with the corresponding Lagrange multiplier
\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
, which convert the Hamiltonian into a homogeneous constraint.
Applied to (
42
), this yields an equivalent homogeneously parameterized action

S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{\,\overline{\!par\!}}[\gamma,\pi,\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu]=\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!+\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}-\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{n}{H}_{n}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{n}\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{}_{,{n}}\big)\,.

(50)

This action is equivalent to (
42
) because
(\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})
is first-class and the correspondence gauge
\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{-\,}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
directly reduces it to the original (
42
). This property is universal
11
11

11

To be precise, the first-class property of the homogeneous parametrization constraint holds for so-called first-class Hamiltonians. However, any local canonical Hamiltonian can be made first-class by shifting it by a certain combination of second-class constraints
[
23
]
, ensuring the existence of a linear combination of first class.

following from the consistency of the dynamical and gauge canonical structures
[
23
]
.
In the second step one introduces to a theory a pair of spatially average-free canonical fields,
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
,
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
, along with a corresponding Lagrange multiplier
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
, via the trivial, pure-gauge combination

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,-\,}\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\big)\,.

(51)

This step localizes the auxiliary sector forming the local field combinations,

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,-\,}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\big)+{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,-\,}\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\big)\;=\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,-\,}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\big)\,,
and leads, in view of
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\,\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\,\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}={\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}
, to the following equivalent representation

S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{par}[\gamma,\pi,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu]=\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\hskip 0.5pt+\hskip 0.5pt}\overline{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{n}{H}_{n}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{n}\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{}_{,{n}}\big)\,.

(52)

So far, the scheme applies to any field theory, but the resulting action is not local, as it still contains the nonlocal term
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}
. A distinctive feature of
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG is the secondary constraint term,
-{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{n}(\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!})_{,{n}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu)\,(\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717ptF_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt)
, which through redefining Lagrange multipliers as
(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu)\to(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{\prime})\,{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\,(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu){\,+\,}\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}^{-1}\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
, incorporates the missing average-free part of the Hamiltonian into

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\hskip 0.5pt+\hskip 0.5pt}\overline{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})
, finally achieving localization (
43
). Without this constraint, localization of the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG action fails. As discussed later, for general GUMG models, localization of (
52
) cannot be completed in the same way, since for nonconstant
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
, the secondary constraint is no longer an average-free part of the local Hamiltonian.

We end this excursus with the note that the local constraint
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\hskip 0.5pt+\hskip 0.5pt}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}
in (
43
) is not first-class, as it decomposes into three constraints:

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\hskip 0.5pt+\hskip 0.5pt}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}=\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\hskip 0.5pt+\hskip 0.5pt}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}+\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+\widetilde{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}\,,

(53)

of which the first two are first-class and form
\mathcal{P}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\!\!(I)}}
, (
48
), while the third, proportional to the secondary constraint, is second-class. A similar discussion applies to the UMG case
[
8
]
; however, it is redundant there because the secondary constraint and the whole (
53
) are first-class.

3.2
Henneaux–Teitelboim-like action

As the next step, we change the basis of constraints in the parameterized action (
43
). The nearest goal is to isolate the dependence on
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
into a single constraint, ensuring that this constraint, along with the momentum constraint and the term
\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}
, form the canonical counterpart of the Einstein-Hilbert term in the Lagrangian action. Other terms should be independent of
\pi^{{m}{n}}
to facilitate a GR-like reduction with respect to gravitational momenta.

It is straightforward to see that the following pairs of constraint conditions are equivalent:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}=0\,,\\
\mathcal{T}_{,{n}}\>\equiv\>\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}(F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!})_{,{n}}=0\,.\\
\end{array}\right.\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}=0\,,\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}_{,{n}}=0\,.\\
\end{array}\right.

In (
43
) such basis change is achieved through a nondegenerate linear transformation of the Lagrange multipliers
(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{\prime{n}})\,\to\,(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu^{n})
:

\begin{array}[]{|lcl}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}&\!=&\!\!\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda-(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)\,,\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{\prime{n}}&\!=&\!\!-\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}^{-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu^{n}\,,\\
\end{array}

(59)

leading to the action in the form
12
12

12

At this step, the overall
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
coefficient in one of the constraints disappears, eliminating the need for special reservations in the exceptional
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}=0
case. (See the remark on this case in Section
2.3
.)

S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{par^{\prime}}[\gamma,\pi,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu]\;\,=\,\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{n}{H}_{n}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}_{,{n}}\big)\,.

(60)

In (
60
), all dependence on gravitational variables is confined in local structures, analogous to those in Einstein’s general relativity.
To establish an accurate correspondence, we rescale Lagrange multiplier
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
, and rename the cosmological-constant field term
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
:

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda=\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{-1}\,,\qquad\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,,

(61)

yielding the yet canonical action

\displaystyle S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{par^{\prime\prime}}\![\gamma,\pi,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu]

\displaystyle\!=\!\!

\displaystyle\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\Big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}({H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{+}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{-1}\!\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{n}{H}_{n}+\big(\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+\,}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\Big).

The factor multiplying
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
is similar to the Hamiltonian constraint (
16
) of general relativity with the combination
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\defeq\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
playing the role of the dynamical cosmological-constant term.
The factor multiplying
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{n}
is the standard GR momentum constraint (
17
).

Since all dependence on
\pi^{{m}{n}}
is confined within the GR-like canonical structures, it can be expressed through their own variational equations of motion,

\displaystyle\pi^{{m}{n}}\eomeq{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}(K^{{m}{n}}-\gamma^{{m}{n}}K)\,,\qquad K_{{m}{n}}=\tfrac{1}{2\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}}(\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}_{{m};{n}}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}_{{n};{m}})\,,

(63)

and reduced from the action in the same manner as in general relativity. Upon reduction, the first three terms on the right-hand side of (
3.2
) transform into Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian with a modified dynamical cosmological term

\displaystyle S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{alt_{0}}[\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}\Big({{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g)-\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\Big)+\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,,

(64)

where
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g_{{\mu}{\nu}}
is the spacetime metric corresponding to the ADM variables
(\gamma_{{m}{n}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{n},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!})
via

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g_{{\mu}{\nu}}d{X}^{{\mu}}d{X}^{{\nu}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\gamma_{{m}{n}}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{m}{+}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}}^{m}d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)(d{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{n}{+}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}}^{n}d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}}^{2}d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\,,
volume density
\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\!\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}
, and
F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}
is explicated as
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}
. Also, we form the Henneaux–Teitelboim spacetime vector field
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
:

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\equiv\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{0},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{m}\big)=\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu^{m}\big)\qquad\Rightarrow\quad\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)=\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}.

(65)

The terms in (
3.2
) and (
64
) proportional to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
are independent of
\pi^{{m}{n}}
and thus remain unaffected by the reduction. In general, these terms introduce a perfect-fluid degree of freedom and break spacetime covariance.
In (
64
) the structure of the last term ensures that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
is an
on-shell constant
, while the apparent noncovariance arises from the modified cosmological-field term.

All action transformations leading to (
64
) for the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG subfamily preserved classical equivalence.
Thus, this alternative representation is equivalent to the original formulation by construction. It is also important to note that the action (
64
) describes models with all
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\in\mathbb{R}
, including the exceptional cases
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
and
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{-}1
.

Another convenient form of the alternative representation is obtained by rescaling
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
:

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\defeq\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}+1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\,,

(66)

so that the alternative action takes the form

\displaystyle S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{alt}[\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}\Big({{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g)-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\Big)+\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\>\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\!\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}+1}\!\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\,,

(67)

where the first integral acquires the form of the Einstein-Hilbert action with the dynamical cosmological constant, while the apparent noncovariance shifts to the latter term
13
13

13

If the metric
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g_{{\mu}{\nu}}
transforms as a tensor and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
transforms as a scalar, the latter term in (
67
) is noncovariant.
Alternatively, if
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}+1}\!\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
is assumed to transform as a scalar, diffeomorphism invariance is instead broken by the cosmological-field term in the first integral.
In any case, covariance is restored for
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{-}1
, corresponding to the Henneaux–Teitelboim action
[
8
]
of
unimodular
gravity.

.

3.3
Dynamical properties

The fields
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
enter the Lagrangian action (
67
) linearly. They are intertwined, so their variational equations determine each other’s on-shell behavior. The variational equation for the auxiliary fields
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
,

\frac{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{alt}}}{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}}}=0\qquad\mapsto\qquad\partial_{\mu}(\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}+1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda)\eomeq 0\,,

(68)

generalizes behavior of the cosmological term, implying that the on-shell spacetime constant is

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}=\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}+1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\eomeq\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,.

(69)

The variation with respect to the cosmological-constant field
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
,

\frac{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{alt}}}{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda}}=0\qquad\mapsto\qquad\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}=\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}+1}\,,

(70)

relates the spacetime metric determinant to the divergence of the auxiliary vector field.

Since
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
enters the action linearly, its dynamical nature ensures that the cosmological term compensates the divergence term on shell. Consequently, the principal function is determined by the values of the Einstein Lagrangian term on classical configurations.
Since
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
appears in the action only through the divergence term
\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
, it is defined by the equations of motion only up to an arbitrary spacetime transverse vector field. This reflects an additional gauge ambiguity in the auxiliary sector.

From the canonical structure of this action, it is also clear that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}=\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
is a gauge-invariant homogeneous constant of motion. It obviously commutes with all constraints of (
60
), ensuring that it is gauge-invariant and preserved in time. Furthermore, due to the constraint
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}_{,{m}}=0
, on shell it does not depend on
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}
. The physical sector of the model naturally foliates into leaves parameterized by constant
\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
, which, in view of the first constraint in (
60
), corresponds to the on-shell values of
\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}\eomeq-\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\,\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
. Consequently, the GR branch corresponds to the leaf
\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}=0
, while the non-GR branch consists of leaves with
\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\neq 0
.

In the ADM parametrization of the metric field, relation (
70
)
can be interpreted as relating
\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
to another nondynamical field — the lapse function — via

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\eomeq\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}
.
At the same time, on the non-GR branch, the lapse function
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
satisfies additional on-shell conditions, since in the canonical framework it serves as a Lagrange multiplier for a mixed-class constraint.
From the Poisson bracket relations (
2.2
), (
3.1
) in the constraint basis of the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG parameterized action (
60
), the second-class constraints are given by
\widetilde{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}
, where
F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}
, and the longitudinal part of
{H}_{n}
. For
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\neq\>}{-}1
the corresponding Lagrange multipliers,
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}}
and
(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N})
, must vanish due to the consistency equations. This,
together with (
70
)
14
14

14

In the context of the canonical action (
60
), equation (
70
) arises from variation with respect to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
, implying the extremal condition:
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda\,{\,\equiv\>}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}\eomeq\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)\,{\,\equiv\>}\,\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
. By separating the average and average-free components and taking into account consistency conditions, one arrives at (
71
).

, leads to

\begin{array}[]{|l}\widetilde{\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}}\,=\,\dot{\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+\,}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)\,\eomeq\,0\,,\\
\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\,\eomeq\,\overline{\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}}\,=\,\dot{\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\;\;\;\text{---\; arbitrary ,}\end{array}\qquad(\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\neq-1\,,\;\;\text{non-GR branch:}\;\;\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\neq 0)\,.

(71)

Summarizing the on-shell behaviour of the parameterized lapse and shift functions, one obtains

\begin{array}[]{|l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\,\eomeq\,\dot{\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}\quad\;\;(\,\Rightarrow\;\;\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{-1}}\,\eomeq\,0\,)\,,\\
(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N})\,\eomeq\,0\,,\end{array}\qquad(\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\neq-1\,,\;\;\text{non-GR branch:}\;\;\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\neq 0)\,.

(72)

Relations (
72
) indicate that the gauge freedom of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
is functionally incomplete, allowing for rescaling by an arbitrary nonzero function of time,
\dot{\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
, and the transverse components of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{n}
remain undetermined by the equations of motion, reflecting the gauge freedom associated with transverse spatial diffeomorphisms.
Notably, the first condition in (
72
) reinstates the restriction condition (
3
) from the original theory, now with an extra factor accounting for homogeneous time reparametrization
15
15

15

The homogeneous reparametrization factor
\dot{\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
reduces to unity in the correspondence gauge (
49
), verifying the result by comparison with the equations of motion of non-parameterized theory.
.

On the GR branch and in the exceptional UMG case, where all constraints are first class, none of the Lagrange multipliers are fixed by the equations of motion

\begin{array}[]{|l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\,\eomeq\,\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}}\;\text{ --- arbitrary}\,,\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{n}\;\text{ --- arbitrary}\,,\end{array}\qquad(\text{for\;}\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}=-1\;\text{or\; on the GR branch:}\;\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}=0)\,.

(73)

The dynamics of gravitational degrees of freedom in general is governed by the variational equations for the spacetime metric
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g_{{\mu}{\nu}}
, which take the form of the Einstein-Hilbert equation

\frac{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{alt}}}{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g_{{\mu}{\nu}}}}\,=\,0\qquad\mapsto\qquad{{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}^{{\mu}{\nu}}\!-\frac{1}{2}{{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g^{{\mu}{\nu}}\>\eomeq\>\frac{1}{2}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}T_{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{{\mu}{\nu}}\,,

(74)

where all terms involving
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
are attributed to the cosmological
matter
sector, contributing to the energy-momentum tensor
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}T_{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{{\mu}{\nu}}
. Using (
70
), the on-shell form of the energy-momentum tensor is

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}T_{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{{\mu}{\nu}}\,\defeq\,\frac{2}{\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}}\frac{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{S^{{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}_{mat}}}{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g_{{\mu}{\nu}}}}\>\eomeq\>\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\,n^{{\mu}}n^{{\nu}}+\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g^{{\mu}{\nu}}\!+n^{{\mu}}n^{{\nu}}\big)\,,

(75)

where
n^{\mu}
is the unit vector field orthogonal to constant-time hypersurfaces. Here the energy density is given by
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varrho\eomeq\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
, and the pressure is
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}p\eomeq\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
, reinstating the perfect fluid equation of state with the constant parameter
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
:

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}p\eomeq\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varrho\,.

(76)

The dynamical behavior of the energy and pressure becomes more explicit when
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
is expressed in terms of the spatially homogeneous constant of motion
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\eomeq\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
, (
61
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{|lcl}\varrho&\eomeq&\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}^{-1}\,\dot{\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,\eomeq\,\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}-1}\,\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,,\\
p&\eomeq&\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}^{-1}\,\dot{\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,\eomeq\,\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}-1}\,\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,.\end{array}

Analogous to Einstein’s general relativity in the presence of cosmological perfect fluid, the trace of the Einstein equations (
74
) takes the form

{{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g)\;{\,\equiv\>}\;2\nabla_{\mu}(n^{\mu}K{-\,}n^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}n^{\mu})+K^{m}_{\,{n}}K^{n}_{\,{m}}-KK+\,R(\gamma)\eomeq\frac{1}{\mathrm{d}{-}2}(\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{+}1-\mathrm{d}\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw})\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\,.

(80)

At the same time, the Lagrangian counterpart of the canonical constraint
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{+}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\eomeq 0
, (
16
), in ADM components is

K^{m}_{\,{n}}K^{n}_{\,{m}}-KK-\,R(\gamma)\eomeq-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\,,

(81)

while the momentum constraint
{H}_{n}\eomeq 0
, (
17
), imposes a GR-like condition on the extrinsic curvature (
131
):

K_{{m}\,;{n}}^{\;{n}}-K_{,{m}}\eomeq 0\,.

(82)

3.4
Canonical gauge structure

In this subsection, we analyze the gauge symmetry on the non-GR branch. The simplifications in the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG case provide clearer expressions for the spatially nonlocal gauge structure and simpler derivation of Lagrangian gauge transformations from canonical ones.

As noted in Section
3.1
, in the parameterized theory (
43
), the independent first-class canonical generators correspond to the constraint (
48
) and the transverse spatial diffeomorphisms:

To highlight the distinction in gauge transformations, we decompose
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda
in (
84
) into its homogeneous and average-free parts,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda=\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}+\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}
. This shows that only the homogeneous part,
\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}
, transforms as the Lagrange multiplier for a first-class constraint, whereas the transformation of the average-free component,
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}
, is on-shell trivial, indicating its second-class nature.

To better understand the origin of the mixed-class gauge behavior of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda
it is useful to examine the transformations of the Lagrange multipliers in the minimally parameterized action (
52
).
There,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
acts as the multiplier for a first-class constraint, transforming as
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon,\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\dot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}
, while
(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu)
is the multiplier for the second-class constraint
-\widetilde{\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}}
, transforming as
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon,\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu){\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{-}\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\,(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N})\overline{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda_{,{k}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta_{{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}(\hskip-1.0ptt\hskip-1.0pt)}}^{k}
.
These correspond to the Lagrange multipliers of (
60
) via

\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
and
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{-}\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu)
, endowing
\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}
with first-class and
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}
with second-class transformation properties.

This structure aligns with the Henneaux–Teitelboim unimodular theory
[
8
]
, the special case (
10
) with
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{-}1
where the longitudinal spatial diffeomorphisms and inhomogeneous constraint
\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt
become first-class. In this case, the first-class constraints
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}\mathcal{P}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\!\!(I)}}
and
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}^{\prime}\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}}
generate a local time-reparametrization symmetry via
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}{+}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
, with the fully functional parameter
\overline{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}{\,+\,}\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}^{\prime}

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
and
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda{\,-\,}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)
transform under their own average-free gauge parameter
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}
. Notably, the transformations of the metric and auxiliary sectors are coupled only through the homogeneous gauge parameter
\overline{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}
, reflecting the decoupling of the gauge-trivial sector of the average-free auxiliary fields:
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}

\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
, and
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}{\,-\,}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)

Lagrangian gauge symmetries

Given the restricted nature of generalized unimodular theory, we expect Lagrangian gauge transformations of the metric fields to take the form of restricted diffeomorphisms of Einstein’s general relativity. A direct way to verify this is by tracking field redefinitions leading to the configuration space of the action (
3.2
) and adjusting gauge parameters to match those generating standard canonical transformations in GR.
This can be done
17
17

17

We comment on this derivation in Appendix
B.1
.
, and ultimately, such GR-like gauge transformations are induced by the canonical generator

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt^{\prime}\!\!}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon,\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta}{(\,.\,)}={\mathchoice{\big\{{\,.\,},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}({H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{+}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{-1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}})}\big\}}{\big\{{\,.\,},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}({H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{+}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip The rearrangement of the full gauge basis

(\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon,\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta_{{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}(\hskip-1.0ptt\hskip-1.0pt)}}^{m},\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\chi_{{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}(\hskip-1.0ptt\hskip-1.0pt)}}^{m})\to(\overline{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon},\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta_{{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}(\hskip-1.0ptt\hskip-1.0pt)}}^{m},\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\chi^{m})
disentangles the internal auxiliary-sector symmetry from that affecting the metric fields.

In this section we discussed the symmetry on the physically interesting non-GR branch,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\propto\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}{\,\neq\>}0
, which describes gravity in the presence of a cosmological perfect fluid. The GR branch,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\propto\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
, retains full diffeomorphism invariance in the metric sector
[

]
. In the canonical treatment this follows from the Poisson bracket matrix of constraints (
2.2
,
3.1
),
which becomes zero-rank on shell for
{\mathcal{T}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}}\propto{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}=0
, equivalent to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
.
Since
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
is gauge-invariant (
97
), the branch division is itself gauge-invariant.

It is also instructive to examine the completeness of the obtained gauge symmetries from the perspective of the restricted-theory approach
[
20
]
.
For a regular restriction of the gauge theory, one expects the restricted theory’s gauge algebra to be a subalgebra of the parental theory’s gauge algebra. The residual symmetries can be identified by solving the explicit linear constraints on the infinitesimal gauge parameters. For the representation (
1
), this was performed in
[
14
]
and led to a discussion of an apparent paradox: a mismatch between naive gauge restriction counting and actual number of canonical symmetries
20
20

20

In the original GUMG approach (
1
), imposing the one-dimensional algebraic restriction (
3
) on a parental theory with a closed gauge algebra could be expected to break at most one local gauge degree of freedom. However, the canonical treatment suggests that on the non-GR branch, two gauge symmetries are broken.

.
This was resolved by the observation that one of the naive residual gauge symmetries violates spacetime locality.

In the alternative representation, a similar investigation of residual symmetries is possible, though the restriction mechanism is a bit subtler. One can vary the alternative
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG action (
64
) under the unrestricted GR gauge transformations (
3.4
), adjusting auxiliary field variations to cancel as many structures as possible while preserving time locality. The anomaly in this variation imposes constraints on the gauge parameters of the parental symmetry.

Changing the metric diffeomorphism transformations also fails to eliminate the anomaly in the matter term. The two anomaly terms in (

appear as the left-hand sides of independent equations of motion on the non-GR branch, (

theories imposes two constraints on the gauge parameters
23
23

23

Note that the first term in the anomaly (
106
) is independent of the on-shell nonvanishing spatially homogeneous part of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{-1}}
, since
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}^{-1}}\,(\partial{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta})\,{\hskip 2.56073pt\overline{\hskip-1.42262pt\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\hskip-1.42262pt}\hskip 2.56073pt}=0
due to the average-free property of the divergence
(\partial{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta})
. Thus, the anomaly reduces solely to terms proportional to the spatially homogeneous part of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
.

:

\begin{array}[]{|l}\,(\partial{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta})=\,0\,,\quad\Rightarrow\;\;{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta}^{m}=\,\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta_{{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}(\hskip-1.0ptt\hskip-1.0pt)}}^{m},\\
\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}=F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}\overline{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}\,.\end{array}

(107)

These correspond to the canonical gauge transformations (
86
): the metric fields transform with the spatially homogeneous parameter
\overline{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}
, while spatial diffeomorphisms are restricted to transverse ones, associated with coordinate transformations of unit Jacobians.
Besides these residual transformations
the auxiliary sector retains the internal symmetry parameterized by
\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\chi^{n}
, which incorporates
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}
from (
86
) via
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{-}(\partial\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\chi)
.

The constraints (
107
) on the parameters of the parental gauge symmetry confirm that all local (in time) symmetries in the physical sector are exhausted by the canonical symmetries of the theory, supporting the conclusions of
[
20
]
regarding the reasons for the additional reduction of the gauge symmetry on the non-GR branch.

On the non-GR branch, where
{\hskip 2.56073pt\overline{\hskip-1.42262pt\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\hskip-1.42262pt}\hskip 2.56073pt}
vanishes, the anomaly disappears, indicating the restoration of full diffeomorphism invariance. In the UMG case, (
10
), where
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{-}1
, the anomaly (
106
) does not arise, consistent with the fact that the Henneaux–Teitelboim action for unimodular gravity retains full diffeomorphism invariance
[
8
]
.

4
Alternative Action for General GUMG Theories

In this section, we extend the derivation of the alternative parameterized action to general GUMG theories with nonconstant
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
. However, certain complications deserve special attention. The local parameterization procedure is generally inapplicable, so we analyze its failure
and use a consistent parameterization scheme, also discussed in Section
3.1
.
This clarifies the origin of
spatial
nonlocality,
which affects the dynamics and further complicates the gauge structure. Note that the description remains local in time, so all nonlocalities discussed in this section are strictly
spatial
nonlocalities.

4.1
Parameterized canonical action

As in
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG theory, we first seek a parameterized model that is physically equivalent to the initial extended canonical theory (
31
). However, for a generic GUMG model, a common field-theoretic issue arises: the naive parameterized action with the local constraints,

S^{loc}_{par}=\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!+\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{m}{H}_{m}\!-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{\prime{m}}{\mathcal{T}}_{,{m}}\big)\,,
fails to be preserve this equivalence.
To analyse the problem, consider the Poisson bracket relations for the new constraint,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\c
