{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{m}{H}_{m}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{m}{H}_{m}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{m}{H}_{m}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{m}{H}_{m},{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})\}}}\;=\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g_{,{n}}\xi^{n}\tfrac{1}{W}\,\mathcal{T}-{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\partial\xi)\,\mathcal{T}\;\;\weq\;\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g_{,{n}}\xi^{n}\frac{W{+}1}{W}\,\overline{\mathcal{T}}\,,\\
{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}{\mathcal{T}}_{,{m}}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}{\mathcal{T}}_{,{m}}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}{\mathcal{T}}_{,{m}}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}{\mathcal{T}}_{,{m}},{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})\}}}\;=\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\partial\eta)\,\Theta\,\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt\,\mathcal{T}+{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(g\hskip 1.0pt\accentset{\leftrightarrow}{\partial}\hskip-1.0pt_{{n}}(\partial\eta)W\big)F^{2}\gamma^{{n}{m}}{H}_{m}\;\;\weq\;\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\partial\eta)\,\Theta\,\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt\,\overline{\mathcal{T}}\,,\\
\end{array}

where the structure
\Theta({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
is defined in (
26
), as before,
\,(\partial\xi)\defeq\xi^{{k}}_{\,,{k}}\,
,

(f\hskip 1.0pt\accentset{\leftrightarrow}{\partial}\hskip-1.0pt_{{n}}g)\defeq(fg_{,{n}}{-}f_{,{n}}g)
, weak equality
\>\weq\>
denotes the equality on the constraint surface, and
f({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}),g({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}),\xi^{m}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}),\eta^{m}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
are arbitrary test functions. These relations extend (
2.2
) to the full set of structure relations for the theory, described by the naive local parameterized action
S^{loc}_{par}
.
The non-equivalence can be noticed for spatial diffeomorphisms
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{m}{H}_{m}
.
The right-hand side of (
2.2
) depends only on the divergence
(\partial\xi)
, demanding that a gauge part of spatial diffeomorphisms should be transverse. However, in the naive parameterized theory
S^{loc}_{par}
, the right-hand side of (
4.1
) introduces dependence on additional components of
\xi^{m}
, implying that not all transverse diffeomorphisms are remain first-class. This signals a modification of the gauge structure and an altered number of degrees of freedom.

A more rigorous and direct proof of the inequivalence between the original GUMG theory
S_{{\scriptscriptstyle E}}
, (
31
), and the naive parameterized model
S^{loc}_{par}
follows from the violation of the equivalence conditions discussed in Section
3.1
. The constraint rank condition and the requirement for an accessible correspondence gauge for
S^{loc}_{par}
reduce to the question of whether a
first-class
constraint of the form
\mathcal{P}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\!\!(I)}}^{loc}\defeq(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}){\,+\,}\alpha^{n}{H}_{n}{\,+\,}\beta\,\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\mathcal{T}\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt
exists, where
\alpha^{n}
and
\beta
are linear operators acting to the right on the constraints.
The involution of
\mathcal{P}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\!\!(I)}}^{loc}
with all constraints

(
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}),{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\xi^{m}{H}_{m},{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{m}{\mathcal{T}}_{,{m}}
)
imposes the following on-shell conditions on
\alpha^{m}
and
\beta
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}{\alpha}^{n}(\tfrac{W+1}{W}f_{,{n}})\,-\,{\beta}(\widetilde{\Theta\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5ptf})\;\;{=}\;\;0\,,\\
{\beta}\big(\widetilde{\Omega\,(\partial\xi)}\big)\;\;{=}\;\;-(\tfrac{W+1}{W}\xi^{n})_{,{n}}\,,\\
{\alpha}^{n}\big(\Omega\,(\partial\eta)\big)_{\!,{n}}\;\;{=}\;\;-\Theta\,\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt\,(\partial\eta)\,.\\
\end{array}\right.

which must hold for
arbitrary
local form factors
f({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}),\xi^{m}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}),\eta^{m}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
.
However, these conditions fail in general. For instance, for transverse
\xi^{m}
:
(\partial\xi){\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
, the second relation imposes a to nontrivial constraint on the phase space variables:
\partial_{n}W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
. Likewise, the first relation imposes additional constraints
24
24

24

The system (
4.1
) admits a solution on the subspace of the constraint surface, where the spatial volume density and the conjugate trace of the momenta are homogeneous:
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\overline{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
,
\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\overline{\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
. On this subspace, the naive parameterization looks consistent, providing the correspondence of the solutions and the gauge structures. However, the Poisson bracket matrix of constraints exhibits a rank discontinuity at this subspace. So, the two theories are inequivalent outside this subspace the theories describing different numbers of degrees of freedom.

.
Since no independent first-class constraint
\mathcal{P}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\!\!(I)}}^{loc}
exists to compensate for the additional phase space variables introduced by parameterization, the number of degrees of freedom changes, and the original nonparameterized action (
31
) cannot be recovered upon the consistent reduction. This confirms the inconsistency of the naive local parameterization
S^{loc}_{par}
.

A possible way to make the local canonical parameterization scheme consistent is to extend the constraints with
O(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}_{,{m}})
terms, which vanish in the correspondence gauge (
49
). These terms can be expressed as a series in powers of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
, and their spatial derivatives. However, in nonlinear gauge field theories with open algebras, such an approach typically leads to infinite series involving auxiliary variables and their derivatives.
Additionally, one could modify the initial Hamiltonian by adding total spatial derivatives. While these do not affect the local properties of the Hamiltonian, they become nontrivial bulk components of a local constraint after the parameterization. However, this does not restore equivalence to the original theory while preserving spacetime locality.

Consistently parameterized action

The discussion in the context of
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG suggests that the key ingredients leading to the action in the Henneaux–Teitelboim-like form are the parameterization of the canonical action with local fields and the separation of a new constraint with a functionally complete
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
structure. These goals can be achieved by a consistent parameterization, which, however, picks up only the homogeneous component of the Hamiltonian into the new constraint, and then mixing it with the specific secondary constraint, which encodes information of the inhomogeneous (average free) component of the Hamiltonian.
As mentioned in Section
3.1
, one can always perform a homogeneous parameterization

S_{\,\overline{\!par\!}}=\int d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}+\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}-\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{m}{H}_{m}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{m}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}\big)

of the canonical theory (
31
), which implements homogeneous time-reparametrization gauge freedom
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t^{\prime}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
and guarantees physical equivalence of the theories
25
25

25

The homogeneously parameterized theory
S_{\,\overline{\!par\!}}
is equivalent to the original one, as it satisfies the rank and correspondence criteria. From general considerations
[
23
]
, there exist a first-class Hamiltonian of the form
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{+}U_{0}^{n}{H}_{n})
, with
U_{0}^{n}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}U_{0}^{n}(\gamma,\pi)
(
2.2
), providing a new first-class homogeneous constraint
\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{+}U_{0}^{n}{H}_{n})
in
S_{\,\overline{\!par\!}}
. This constraint is evidently transversal to the correspondence gauge
\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{-\,}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
, guaranteeing equivalence with the original theory. The theory based on
S_{\,\overline{\!par\!}}
and its Lagrangian version are briefly discussed in Appendix
C
.

.
Furthermore, one can localize the sector of the auxiliary fields by adding to the action the pure gauge combination of spatially inhomogeneous fields,

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,-\,}\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi}_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\big)\,,
which remains decoupled until the basis of constraints is changed.
By aggregating the average and average-free components of the auxiliary fields into local fields
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
, we obtain the consistently parameterized action

\displaystyle S_{par}[\gamma,\pi,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu]\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\Big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!+\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{m}{H}_{m}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{m}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}\Big),\;\quad

(116)

which is physically equivalent to the GUMG extended action (
31
). The new parametrization constraint contains only the spatially average component
\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
of the original Hamiltonian density term.
However, this form of the constraint keeps the equivalence transparent. The criterion for equivalent parameterization is the introduction of a new first-class constraint, which is transversal to the correspondence gauge condition.
From general arguments
[
23
]
, the original theory (
31
) admits a first-class Hamiltonian of the form
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{+}U_{0}^{n}{H}_{n})
with
U_{0}^{n}=U_{0}^{n}(\gamma,\pi)
given by (
2.2
)
26
26

26

These
U_{0}^{n}(\gamma,\pi)
correspond to on-shell solutions for the Lagrange multipliers for the primary constraints.

.
This promotes a new first-class constraint in the consistently parameterized theory (
116
):

\mathcal{P}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\!\!(I)}}\defeq\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{+}U_{0}^{n}{H}_{n}}\,.

(117)

This constraint is clearly transversal to the correspondence gauge condition,

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t=0\,.

(118)

However, its transversality to a local constraint is ensured by the locality of the
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
term in (
117
), meaning that the (non)locality of the Hamiltonian term, which depends on non-auxiliary metric fields, does not affect this property.

Constraint structure

The constraint basis of the parameterized action (
116
), in addition to the original constraints
({H}_{n},\mathcal{T}_{,{n}})
of (
31
), includes the new constraint
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,+\,}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}
. Consequently, the original structure relations (
2.2
) are supplemented by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}&{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}),{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})\}}}\;=\;0\,,\\
&{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}^{n}{H}_{n},{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})\}}}\;=\;-{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\partial\xi)\overline{g}\,\mathcal{T}\;\weq\;0\,,\\
&{\mathchoice{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\big\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\big\}}{\{{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}},{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})}\}}{\{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\eta^{{m}}\mathcal{T}_{,{m}},{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}g(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})\}}}\;=\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\partial\eta)\overline{g}\,\Theta\,\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt\,\mathcal{T}+{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big((\partial\eta)W\big)_{\!,{n}}\overline{g}\,F^{2}\gamma^{{n}{k}}{H}_{k}\;\weq\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\partial\eta)\overline{g}\,\Theta\,\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt\,\overline{\mathcal{T}}\,,\\
\end{array}

where
\Theta({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
is defined in (
26
). As expected, only the homogeneous component of the test function
g
appears on the right-hand sides.
Due to the latter Poisson bracket, the new constraint
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,+\,}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}
is not first-class. However, structure relations (
2.2
) and (
4.1
) imply that the system of involution conditions with basis constrains for the coefficients
\alpha
and
\beta
in the combination
\mathcal{P}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\!\!(I)}}\defeq(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}){\,+\,}\alpha^{m}{H}_{m}{\,+\,}\beta\,\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\mathcal{T}\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt
now has a solution given by (
117
).
The basis of the first-class constraint algebra for consistently parameterized theory (
116
) is spanned by
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f\mathcal{P}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\!\!(I)}}
and transverse spatial diffeomorphisms
\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\xi}_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{m}{H}_{m}\,
with
{\xi_{{\scriptscriptstyle(t)}}^{\,{m}}}\vphantom{|\xi}_{,{m}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
, (
36
). As in the original theory, the longitudinal component of spatial diffeomorphisms and the secondary constraint can be treated as second-class basis elements.

4.2
Henneaux–Teitelboim-like action and delocalization

Delocalization operator

Starting from the consistently parameterized action (
116
) we modify the constraint basis to consolidate the
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
structure in a single constraint. The set of constraint equations from representation (
116
):
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{,{m}}\equiv(WF{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!})_{,{m}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
,
describes the same constraint surface as the set

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}{+}W^{-1}\widetilde{WF{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
,
{\mathcal{T}}_{,{m}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
.
The first constraint in the new set can be reformulated as
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
, where the operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
acts on the function
F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
with kernel

E(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})=\delta^{\mathrm{d}{-}1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})-W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)}}W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})+\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}{(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)}}\,,

(123)

where
W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}){\,\equiv\>}W(\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}{\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{$(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})$}}})
and the volume normalization factor is
27
27

27

For the case of noncompact spatial sections, the volume normalization factors can be understood through a limiting procedure. See Appendix
D
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\defeq{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t=const}1
. To clarify the structure, note that in terms of the complementary symmetric projectors
{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}
and
{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}
, which extract the spatially homogeneous (average) and inhomogeneous (average-free) parts of a function respectively, the operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
is represented as

\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt\;{=}\;{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}+W^{-1}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}W\;{=}\;{\hskip 1.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral{I}\hskip 1.0pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt}+{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}-W^{-1}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}W\,,
where
{\hskip 1.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral{I}\hskip 1.0pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}+{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}
is the identity operator.
Consequently, the action of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
on local function
f({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
,
g({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
to the left and right can be expressed as

\begin{array}[]{|l}f\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt\>=\>f-\overline{fW^{-1}}\,W+\overline{f}\,,\\
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptg\>=\>g-W^{-1}\,\overline{Wg}+\overline{g}\,,\\
\end{array}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptg\>=\>{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(fg-\overline{fW^{-1}\!}\,\,\overline{Wg}+\overline{f}\,\overline{g}\big)\,.

(124)

Relevant properties of the operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
are detailed in Appendix
A.1
. The crucial property is that
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
is a
nondegenerate
(full-rank) operator. This nondegeneracy ensures the existence of the inverse operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
(see lemma
A.1
) with the kernel

E^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\;=\;\delta({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\,-\,\frac{W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{\big|}}\,-\,\frac{W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{\big|}\,}\,+\,2\frac{W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{\big|}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\frac{W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{\big|}\,}\,.

(125)

The existence of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
allows expressing
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
in terms of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
on the constraint surface:
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}=-F^{-1}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
, eliminating it from the secondary constraint (
20
).

To form a functionally complete term with
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
in the parametrization constraint, one could handle a more general linear combination with the secondary constraint. This choice does not affect the physical results. The unit coefficient at the parametrization constraint
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}
can be fixed by redefining
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
, so the resulting constraint,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt_{\!a}{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}
, is parameterized by the nondegenerate operator
28
28

28

Properties of more generic
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
-type operators are outlined in Appendix
A.2
. We will further comment on the independence of physical results from the choice of
a
in
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt_{\!a}
when discussing the dynamics, serving as a partial consistency check.

\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt_{\!a}\,\defeq{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}+a^{-1}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}W
,
with a generic sign-definite function
a
.
Our choice of
a{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
is a matter of convenience: when
W{\,\equiv\>}\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}const
, the operators
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
and
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
become the identity operator, recovering the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG results from Section
3
. In the quantum context, such choice is advantageous as their diagonals,
{E}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
and
{E}^{-1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
, reduce to the field-independent spatial
\delta(0)
, and their determinants acquire simpler forms
29
29

29

See Section
4.5
.
.

The explicit spatial nonlocality encoded in the operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
arises in the canonical constraint of the parameterized GUMG theory with nonconstant
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
. This nonlocality stems from the inability to merge the functionally incomplete
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
contributions from the parameterization and secondary constraints into a local structure. Furthermore, one cannot redefine the phase space fields to achieve locality of the canonical action. The secondary constraint restricts the average-free part of the Hamiltonian density
F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
weighted by
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
, while the consistent parameterization promotes the Hamiltonian density to the unweighted homogeneous structure
\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}
. However, local gluing becomes possible in the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG models when
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})\to const
.

A further simplification of the constraint basis is achieved by eliminating
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
via the substitution
-F^{-1}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}
in the secondary constraint
\mathcal{T}_{,{m}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
. This leads to the cumbersome constraint
(W\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}})_{,{m}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
, which can be replaced with the simpler, yet equivalent, constraint constraint
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}_{,{m}}=0
. This equivalence follows from the properties of the operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
(see Corollary
A.1.2
).
The nontrivial equivalence between
(W\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}})_{,{m}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}_{,{m}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
is significant, as it allows us to gather various auxiliary fields into the covariant spacetime divergence,
\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
, in the alternative action (
7
) instead of dealing with a noncovariant, spatially nonlocal metric-dependent differential structure.

To summarize the above discussion, the constraint basis of (
116
) is rearranged:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}=0\,,\\
{H}_{m}=0\,,\\
{(WF{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!})_{,{m}}}=0\,,\\
\end{array}\right.\qquad\to\qquad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}=0\,,\\
{H}_{m}=0\,,\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}_{,{m}}=0\,,\\
\end{array}\right.

(126)

allowing the parameterized canonical action to be equivalently expressed as

\displaystyle S_{par^{\prime}}[\gamma,\pi,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu]\;=\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\Big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!+\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}{+}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{m}{H}_{m}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu^{m}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}_{,{m}}\Big).\;\;

(127)

The equivalence of representations (
116
) and (
127
) is guaranteed by the freedom of choosing any equivalent constraint basis. Alternatively, the change of the constraint basis (
126
) corresponds to the nondegenerate redefinition of the Lagrange multipliers
30
30

30

Transformation (
126
) differs from the Lagrange multiplier redefinition in
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG (
59
), due to the distinct definition of the parametrization constraint in the naive and consistent parametrization schemes.

(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu^{m})\;\to\;(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu^{m})

in (
116
):

\begin{array}[]{|cclc}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}&=&\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda-(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)\,,\\
(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu)&=&-\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda W^{-1}}\,.\\
\end{array}

(128)

In the action (
127
), all appearances of the gravitational momenta
\pi^{{n}{m}}
within the constraints are confined to the GR structures
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
and
{H}_{{n}}
(
16
,
17
).
This comes at the cost of spatial delocalization, which is fully encoded in the operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
. However, this nonlocality is not an artifact of parameterization. Rather, it was implicitly present in the interplay between the dynamical and constraint structures of the original canonical theory (
31
). Consistent parameterization simply made this nonlocality explicit.

Lagrangian form of the parameterized GUMG action

The remaining steps toward the alternative GUMG action in the Henneaux–Teitelboim-like form (
7
) are analogous to those discussed in Section
3.2
for
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG models.
We rescale the Lagrange multiplier
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
by introducing
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF
to purify the
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
term in the constraint and rename
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
to highlight its role as the cosmological-constant field:

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda\>=\>\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}F^{-1}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\,,\qquad\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\>=\>\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,,

(129)

where operators
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
and
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
act on functions to the left.
In terms of the new variables, the action (
3.2
)
becomes

S_{par^{\prime\prime}}[\gamma,\pi,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\!,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu]\;=\;\!\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\Big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}({H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}{+}F^{-1}\!\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\!\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}})-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{m}{H}_{m}\!+\big(\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,\Big).\;\;\vskip-11.38109pt

(130)

Since all dependence on the momenta
\pi^{{m}{n}}
is confined within the GR canonical structure

\,\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{m}{H}_{m}\!\,
,
they can be expressed through their own variational equations,

\displaystyle\pi^{{m}{n}}\eomeq{\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}(K^{{m}{n}}-\gamma^{{m}{n}}K)\,,\qquad K_{{m}{n}}=\tfrac{1}{2\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}}(\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}_{{m};{n}}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}_{{n};{m}})\,,

(131)

and removed from the action as in general relativity, converting the canonical structure into the Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian. The Lagrangian GUMG action thus takes the form

\displaystyle S_{alt_{0}}[\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}\Big({{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g)-\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}\!F^{-1}\!\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\!\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\Big)+\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\;,\quad

(132)

where
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g_{{\mu}{\nu}}
is the spacetime metric corresponding to the ADM variables
(\gamma_{{m}{n}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{m},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!})
via

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g_{{\mu}{\nu}}d{X}^{{\mu}}d{X}^{{\nu}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\gamma_{{m}{n}}(d{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{m}{+}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}}^{m}d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)(d{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{n}{+}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}}^{n}d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}}^{2}d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\,,
the volume density
\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}
emerged from
{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}\!\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}
, and
{{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g)
is the spacetime scalar curvature.
The cosmological matter terms in (
130
), proportional to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
, remain unaffected by the reduction.
For
W{\,\neq\>}{-}1
, these terms break spacetime covariance.
The Henneaux–Teitelboim auxiliary spacetime vector field

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\equiv\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{0},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{m}\big){\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}},\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu^{m}\big)\,,
(
65
),
aggregates in the covariant divergence
\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{\,+\,}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)
. This combination, appearing in the last term of (
132
), ensures that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
is a spacetime constant on shell.

Another convenient form of the alternative representation arises from redefinition
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\to\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
:

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\defeq\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\,,

(133)

where
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
, (
123
), acts to the right,
leading to the alternative action (
7
),

\displaystyle S_{alt}[\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}\,\big({{}^{{}^{\mathrm{d}}\!\!}R}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g)-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\big)+\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF\!\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\,.

(134)

The first term takes the form of the Einstein-Hilbert action with the dynamical cosmological constant
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
. The apparent noncovariance shifts to the second term, which now confines model specific characteristic functions
F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
and
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
(within
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
).
For the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG subfamily (
9
), the correspondence with (
67
) is achieved with
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt\to 1
. In the UMG exceptional case (
10
),
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\to 1
restoring spacetime covariance of the action.

All intermediate transformations of the GUMG theory actions leading to (
132
) and (
134
) preserve classical equivalence, ensuring that the alternative representation is equivalent to the original formulation.

4.3
Dynamical properties

The structure of equations of motion is similar to that in
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG, except for the presence of spatial nonlocality.
The variational equations with respect to the auxiliary fields
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}

\frac{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{S_{alt}}}{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}}}\eomeq 0\qquad\mapsto\qquad\partial_{\mu}(\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda)\;\equiv\;\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\eomeq 0\quad\Rightarrow\quad\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,\eomeq\,0\,,

(135)

generalize the behavior of the cosmological constant, implying that the combination
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\equiv\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
, (
133
), is a spacetime constant on shell.
The operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
, (
124
), introduces spatial nonlocality into the classical relation between the spatial measure factor
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}
and the cosmological-constant field
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
in the representation (
134
):

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\eomeq\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}F^{-1}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}=\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}F^{-1}\frac{W^{-1}}{\,\overline{\!W^{-1}\!}\,\vphantom{I^{|^{I}}}}\,\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,,

(136)

where
\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
is an on-shell constant value of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
,  and
\overline{\!W^{-1}\!}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)
denotes the spatial average of the
W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})
over a
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}const
hypersurface.
This result follows from the properties
31
31

31

For the more general delocalization operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt_{\!a}\,\equiv{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}\!+a^{-1}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}W
, mentioned in Section
4.2
, the on-shell expression would be
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda{\,\eomeq\,}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}F^{-1}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt_{\!a}^{-1}\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
. However, according to Corollary
A.5.1
, this again simplifies to
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}F^{-1}\frac{W^{-1}}{\,\overline{\!W^{-1}\!}\,\vphantom{I^{|^{I}}}}\,\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
from the right-hand side of (
136
), verifying the independence of the choice of
a
.

(
A.1
) of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
.
For the
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG subfamily with
W=\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}const
, the nonlocal factor
{W^{-1}}/{\,\overline{\!W^{-1}\!}}
reduces to unity, reproducing the results of Section
3.3
.
In the unimodular case, where
F({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}}){\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}
and
W{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}{-}1
, the cosmological-constant field
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
remains equal to constant
\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
on shell.

The variational equation with respect to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
,

\frac{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{S_{alt}}}{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda}}\eomeq 0\qquad\mapsto\qquad\sqrt{|\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}g|}\eomeq\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}\,,

(137)

relates the determinant of the spacetime metric to the nonlocally weighted divergence of the Henneaux–Teitelboim auxiliary field (the operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
acts to the left on
\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
), where the weight depends on the induced metric determinant
\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}
.

In terms of the ADM parameterization of the metric, one can express the lapse function of the covariant metric as
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8ptF
, or relate the delocalized analog of the initial restriction condition to the divergence of the auxiliary field:
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}F^{-1}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
, where the operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
acts to the left. In view of the redefinition (
129
), the left-hand side of the latter equality is nothing but the Lagrange multiplier
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda
,
reinstating the on-shell relation

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda{\;\eomeq\;}\dot{}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu){\;\equiv\;}\partial_{\mu}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}\,,

(138)

from the canonical action (
127
). However,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda
is a Lagrange multiplier associated with the mixed-class constraint
32
32

32

Only the spatially homogeneous component of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda
remains undetermined, corresponding to the first-class constraint (
117
). The non-homogeneous part vanishes on shell:
\widetilde{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda W^{-1}}\eomeq 0
, as required by the Dirac consistency conditions for the second-class constraints in the canonical system (
127
).
.
By taking into account the on-shell constraints on the Lagrange multipliers associated with second-class constraints, one can further refine the relation.

A systematic approach to tracking the conditions imposed on canonical Lagrange multipliers is to start from the emerging conditions in the consistently parameterized action (
116
) and follow the redefinitions of the Lagrange multipliers. From the structure relations (
2.2
) and (
4.1
), it follows that the longitudinal spatial diffeomorphisms and the secondary constraint are independent second-class constraints. The consistency conditions then imply

\begin{array}[]{|lcl}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N})&\eomeq&\!\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}}\,\widetilde{U}_{0}\,\equiv\,\overline{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\lambda^{{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}}\big(\Omega^{-1}\Theta\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt-\Omega^{-1}\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\Omega^{-1}\Theta\hskip 0.5pt\pi\hskip 0.5pt}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\Omega^{-1}}\big)\,,\\
(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\mu)&\eomeq&\!0\,,\\
\end{array}

(139)

where
\widetilde{U}_{0}(\gamma,\pi)
is defined in (
30
), and the structures
\Omega({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
and
\Theta({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
are introduced in (
26
).
Under the basis transformation

4.4
Gauge structure

The canonical treatment reveals the following first-class constraints on the non-GR branch for the consistently parameterized GUMG action (
116
),

\begin{array}[]{|lll}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\varepsilon(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}+\overline{F{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}+\overline{U_{0}^{n}{H}_{n}})\,,\\
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta_{{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}(\hskip-1.0ptt\hskip-1.0pt)}}^{m}{H}_{m}\,,\\
\end{array}

(148)

where
U_{0}^{n}
is the particular on-shell solution for the Lagrange multipliers
{N}^{n}
(
2.2
), and
\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta_{{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}(\hskip-1.0ptt\hskip-1.0pt)}}^{m}
is the transverse vector gauge parameter, satisfying
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta_{{\color[rgb]{0.2,0.2,0.2}(\hskip-1.0ptt\hskip-1.0pt)}}^{\,{k}}}\vphantom{|\xi}_{,{k}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
.
The spatial nonlocality of the gauge structure makes the analysis more intricate than in
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG, particularly due to the nonlocal nature of
U_{0}^{n}
. However, this is mainly a technical and presentational complication; structurally, the gauge transformations remain similar to those in
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}
-GUMG.

Rather than following the laborious approach of Section
3.4
, which tracks the gauge transformations under field redefinitions (some of which are spatially nonlocal), we adopt a kind of gauge-restriction approach from the outset. Assuming canonical symmetries, we perturb the action (
130
) under unrestricted canonical transformations generated by
all
constraints. We then examine the anomaly to determine the constraints on transformation parameters and identify the residual free parameters corresponding to gauge symmetry
36
36

36

Restricting general canonical transformations by anomaly-free condition here can be interpreted as examining gauge restrictions on the GR gauge transformations, motivated by the restricted-theory approach.
.

Consider the variation of the action (
130
), decomposed into GR and auxiliary matter parts:

\displaystyle\;=\;\;\!\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\pi^{{m}{n}}\dot{\gamma}_{{m}{n}}\!-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{m}{H}_{m}\!\big)+\!\!\int\!d\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t\hskip 1.0ptd{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}\,\big(\,\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}}+(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)-\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}F^{-1}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\big)\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}\,,\;\;

The first integral in this expression is the nonvanishing contribution from the GR part of the action, which is due to the extension of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}\pi^{{m}{n}}
proportional to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
.
The second integral originates from metric variations of the matter part: the first term incorporates
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{N}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
, while the second accounts for phase-space fields variations. The third integral results from the canonical transformations of auxiliary variables, reducing to
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}\big(\dot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\tau}^{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)\big)\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
, given that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}}{\,\hskip 1.0pt=\>}0
. The underlined terms with time derivatives proportional to
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\zeta}^{\!{\hskip 0.8pt}\scriptscriptstyle{\perp}\!}
cancel.

All terms on the right-hand side of (
152
) are linear in
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
, as expected
38
38

38

Pure GR variation of the GR part of the action vanishes due to the parental gauge symmetry, while the extension of
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}\pi^{{n}{m}}
beyond canonical
GR
transformations is proportional to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
. The variation of the matter part is proportional to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
because its integrand is proportional to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
, and the variation of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
itself is trivial.

.
The role of
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0,0.2}\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
is to cancel other nonvanishing terms proportional to
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
. If
(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)
were a functionally complete object, its variation could fully compensate for all other terms, preventing any anomaly. However, due to the average-free nature of
(\partial\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\nu)
, only contributions proportional to average-free component of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}
can be compensated. Consequently, for

Corollary

shows that the equation

. Thus, the requirement for the anomaly (
