Appendix D
Noncompact Spatial Sections and Delocalization

Spacetime manifolds with noncompact spatial sections
\Sigma_{t}
have important cosmological implications. Therefore, it is worth verifying that results obtained in the compact case remain applicable to noncompact settings. A potential challenge arises from the singular nature of spatial integration in noncompact spaces, particularly when dealing with integral operators.
A full rigorous treatment would require a careful specification of asymptotic boundary conditions compatible with the theory’s dynamics and gauge structure — an analysis that deserves a separate discussion. Nevertheless, we outline the key issues below and demonstrate the applicability of our framework with delocalization operators to a broad class of fields.

On the non-GR branch, where the diffeomorphism symmetry is broken down to foliation-preserving transverse diffeomorphisms, the natural asymptotic conditions for field configurations on noncompact spatial backgrounds are asymptotically constant:

\varphi^{i}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\big|_{|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}|\to\infty}=\,\varphi_{*}^{i}(t)
.
Since we are primarily concerned with infrared aspects of the formalism, we
assume that such fields are bounded functions on
\Sigma_{t}
46
46

46

Formally, this assumption may exclude black hole–like solutions. However, boundedness is adopted here solely as a technical simplification.

.
These asymptotic conditions capture physically interesting cosmological vacua and align with the residual local symmetry of the non-GR branch where spatial nonlocality becomes significant. This motivates the following decomposition:

\varphi^{i}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})=\varphi_{*}^{i}(t)+{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi^{i}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\,,\qquad\varphi^{i}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\big|_{|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}|\to\infty}\!\!\!\!\to\varphi_{*}^{i}(t)\,,\qquad{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi^{i}\big|_{|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}|\to\infty}\!\!\!\!=O\big(\tfrac{1}{|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}|^{c_{i}}}\big)\,\to\,0\,,

(224)

where
asymptotic backgrounds

\varphi_{*}^{i}(t)
are spatially constant functions, and ‘‘
perturbations
’’
47
47

47

While
{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi^{i}
are not small in the bulk (only bounded), they act as small corrections at large distances. Thus, although both components can be locally of the same order, they behave differently under integration.

{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi^{i}
decay at infinity with some falloff rates
c_{i}
.
Regular local functions of such fields inherit the same structure: asymptotic values can be decoupled allowing a similar decomposition:

\mathcal{A}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})=\mathcal{A}_{*}(t)+{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\,,\qquad\mathcal{A}_{*}=\mathcal{A}(\varphi^{i}_{*})\,,\qquad{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}={\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}O}({\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi^{i})\,,

(225)

where the latter represents the asymptotically decaying component.

Even when focusing on local properties of fields and observables: solutions of their equations of motion, gauge transformations etc. the presence of spatial nonlocalities in the action necessitates examining integrated expressions. A key issue arises because integration over noncompact spaces is intrinsically singular for the bounded class of fields. While local theories handle this and derive local properties consistently, additional spatial nonlocalities can compromise this consistency introducing divergent or ambiguous contributions to local expressions.
Below we argue that the nonlocal components of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
and
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
act as perturbations of the ultralocal operator in the integrated quantities and unambiguously contribute to local quantities in the theory.

To handle integration over noncompact spatial manifolds and control possible singularities, we adopt a finite-volume regularization scheme. The constant-time spatial slice
\Sigma_{t}
is truncated to a large but finite subregion of characteristic radius
R
— for example, a
(\mathrm{d}{-}1)
-dimensional ball
|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}|<R
— and all spatial integrations are preformed over this finite domain:

{\mathop{\mathrm{reg}}{}_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}}}:\qquad{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\ldots\;\to\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\,}\ldots\defeq{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}_{|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}|<R}\ldots\,,\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}\;\to\;{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\,}\defeq{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\,}1\,,

(226)

with the regulator
R
ultimately taken to infinity
48
48

48

Alternatively, one could use smooth smearing functions or regularize in Fourier space. However, since our analysis does not require repeated integration by parts, a simple cutoff using a characteristic function suffices. This approach also permits direct analogs of the averaging projectors used in the compact case.

.

Integrals of bounded asymptotically constant
local
functions, (
225
), decompose as

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}\;=\;\mathcal{A}_{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}+{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}\;=\;\mathcal{A}_{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}\,+\mathcal{A}_{1*}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi+\mathcal{A}_{2*}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi+\mathcal{A}_{3*}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi+\ldots\,,

(227)

where the terms fall into different divergence classes with respect to the regularization. The integral over the decaying perturbation part integrates terms, proportional to at least one
{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi
, and is thus subleading compared to the volume-divergent contribution
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}_{*}={\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}\mathcal{A}_{*}
originating from the asymptotic background,
\mathcal{A}_{*}=\mathcal{A}(\varphi_{*})
. Integrals with more decaying factors have better convergence and eventually become finite.
Bilinear combinations decompose analogously:

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}\,\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}_{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}\mathcal{B}_{*}+\mathcal{A}_{*}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\!{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{B}+{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\!{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{*}+{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\!{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{B}\,,
with the leading volume-divergent contribution
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B})_{*}=\mathcal{A}_{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}\mathcal{B}_{*}
and terms with decaying components giving subleading contributions.

In parameterized GUMG, integrations involving spatial nonlocal operators
take the form

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\,\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral C\pdfliteral}\,\mathcal{A}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{0}\,+\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{1}\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{3}}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{2}}+\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{4}\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{6}\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{8}}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{5}\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{7}}+\ldots

(228)

with a finite number of nonlocal contributions, where
\mathcal{B}^{a}
are bounded local integrands. The balanced number of integrations in each nonlocal term is expected since
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
-type operators appear as a sum of projectors at most multiplied by a local regular bounded function. This guarantees that the divergence rate of each term is at most that of a single integration of local structure.

More importantly, nonlocal terms in (
228
) give unambiguous finite contributions to variationally derived local expressions, such as equations of motion, gauge transformations, Noether identities etc. This is because the variational derivative acts only on a one integral factor at a time, while the remaining undifferentiated integral factors compensate the divergencies between numerator and denominator. Resulting nonlocal factors at local quantities
b^{a}_{i}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\equiv\frac{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{\mathcal{B}^{a}}}{{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt}{\varphi^{i}(t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}}
are ratios of asymptotic values of integrands. For example,

\tfrac{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt{}}{\operatorname{\delta}\hskip-1.0pt{\varphi^{i}}}\,\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{1}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{3}}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{2}}\;=\;b^{1}_{i}\,\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{3}}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{2}}-b^{2}_{i}\,\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{1}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{3}}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{2}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{2}}+b^{3}_{i}\,\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{1}}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{2}}\;=\;b^{1}_{i}\,\tfrac{\mathcal{B}^{3}_{*}}{\mathcal{B}^{2}_{*}}-b^{2}_{i}\,\tfrac{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{*}\mathcal{B}^{3}_{*}}{\mathcal{B}^{2}_{*}\mathcal{B}^{2}_{*}}+b^{3}_{i}\,\tfrac{\mathcal{B}^{1}_{*}}{\mathcal{B}^{2}_{*}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}o(1)}

(229)

up to irrelevant contributions
{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}o(1)}
which vanish when regularization is removed. These terms are the same local quantities
b^{a}_{i}
multiplied by nonlocal factors of the type

\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{B}^{1}}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}^{2}}\,,\;\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{1}}}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(\mathcal{B}^{2}_{*}+{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{B}^{2})}-\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\mathcal{B}^{1}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}\mathcal{B}^{2}_{*}}\,,\;...\;\sim\;{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}O}(\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}})={\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}o(1)}\,.

(230)

This observation confirms the validity of the analyses of the gauge structure and equations of motion in the alternative formulation of GUMG, given in the main part, for noncompact spatial backgrounds. To further support this, we examine the specific properties of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
and
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
in more detail.
For this purpose, we reconsider the notion of spatial averaging for local functions and define corresponding average and average-free projectors with well-defined properties within the adopted regularization.
The symmetric averaging projector
{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}
:
{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}\mathcal{A}={\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}\mathcal{A}
is defined via

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}\mathcal{B}\;\defeq\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}\,\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}\;\defeq\;\!\lim_{{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\to\infty}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\,}\mathcal{A}\,{\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\,}}}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\,}\mathcal{B}\,.

(231)

and acts on local bounded functions of asymptotically constant fields as

\overline{\mathcal{A}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\;\defeq\;{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}\mathcal{A}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\;=\;1{\cdot}\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}\defeq\mathcal{A}_{*}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)+\overline{{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\,,

(232)

producing the normalized integrated value. In the noncompact case, the average of the perturbative part,
\overline{{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}}\defeq 1{\cdot}\!\lim_{{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\to\infty}\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\,}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}_{\!\!{\scriptscriptstyle{R}}\,}}
, give the difference between the
\overline{\mathcal{A}}(t)
and the background value
\mathcal{A}_{*}(t)
and vanish as
R\to\infty
. Although it is irrelevant locally, it should be retained until after integrations or functional variations are taken.

The complementary projector
{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}\defeq{\hskip 1.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral{I}\hskip 1.0pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt}-{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}
:
{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}\mathcal{A}={\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}\mathcal{A}
, is defined by

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}\mathcal{B}\;\defeq\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}\,\mathcal{B}-{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}\,\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{B}\,,

(233)

and extracts the integral-free (average-free) part of a function:

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\;\defeq\;{{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}}\mathcal{A}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\;\defeq\;\mathcal{A}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})-\overline{\mathcal{A}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\,,\qquad{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}\mathcal{A}=0\,.

(234)

The integral-free components necessarily decay at spatial infinity and can be represented as divergences of bounded vector fields with appropriate asymptotic behavior.

We are now ready to specify and analyze properties of the delocalization operators
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
and
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
, (
123
), (
125
), in the context of noncompact
\Sigma_{t}
.
The parameterization procedure of Section
4.1
applies analogously, yielding a nonlocal operator at
{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}
with the same integral kernel (
123
)

E(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\;=\;\delta^{\mathrm{d}{-}1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})-W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}{}}W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})+\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}\,.

(235)

This operator is the sum of the identity operator and the nonlocal correction by two projector-type terms, each of order
O(\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}})
. When acting on a bounded asymptotically constant function to either side (i.e. after integrating the kernel with such a function) local and nonlocal contributions become locally of order
O(1)
.
However, decomposing
W
and
W^{-1}
into asymptotic and decaying parts, cf. (
225
), reveals the cancellation of the asymptotic contributions in the nonlocal terms, leaving them with at least one decaying factor. The kernel thus takes the form:

E(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\>=\>\delta^{\mathrm{d}{-}1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})-{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}W_{*}-W^{-1}_{*}\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})-{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\,,

(236)

suggesting that the nonlocal part is the perturbative correction to the unit operator

\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt\defeq\>{\hskip 1.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral{I}\hskip 1.0pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt}+{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt\,.

(237)

When acting on a bounded asymptotically constant function,
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt\,\mathcal{B}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\equiv{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime}}E(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\mathcal{B}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})
,

\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt\,\mathcal{B}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\;=\;\mathcal{B}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\,-\,{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\,\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}W_{*}\mathcal{B}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}-W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W\mathcal{B}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}\,,

(238)

the nonlocal correction yields two types of contributions a decaying
O({\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi)
term, and a suppressed
O({\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\varphi/{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V})
term, which can be neglected in local expressions (e.g., equations of motion).

As noted in Section
4.2
, there is freedom in defining
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
within a family of invertible operators
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt_{\!a}\,\defeq{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\overline{\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.4pt}\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.0pt}+a^{-1}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}W
. In nonlocal case, our choice, (
123
),
is convenient due to effective disentangling of the leading local operator (here — identical operator) from the nonlocal correction
{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
, facilitating the analysis of regularity and asymptotic behavior.

Moreover, the nonlocal part of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
exhibits even more improved behavior, which is easier seen when integrated with two arbitrary functions.
In
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}\,{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt\,\mathcal{B}
the subleading but potentially diverging terms
49
49

49

Small perturbations of the spatial volume density are related to GUMG massive extra degree of freedom
[
14
]
, which suggest that
{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}
acquires a short-range behavior making integrations with
{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W
and
{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}
finite.

O({\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|})
come from
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}
and
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W\mathcal{B}
(multiplied by bounded factors)

\begin{array}[]{lllll}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}\,{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt\,\mathcal{B}&\!\!=&{-}\mathcal{A}_{*}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\left({\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}W_{*}\!+\!{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W\,W^{-1}_{*}\right)\mathcal{B}_{*}-{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}W_{*}\mathcal{B}_{*}-\mathcal{A}_{*}W^{-1}_{*}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{B}+...\,,\end{array}

(239)

where dots denote irrelevant terms vanishing in local variational expressions in
R{\to}\infty
limit.
Asymptotical behavior of
{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}
:

{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}=\tfrac{1}{W_{*}+{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W}-W^{-1}_{*}=-\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W}{W^{2}_{*}}+\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W}{W^{3}_{*}}+O({\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{3})
, is defined by that of
{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W
, which improves the first term in the right hand side of (
239
):

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big({\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}W_{*}\!+\!{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W\,W^{-1}_{*}\big)\;=\;-{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\big(\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W}{W^{3}_{*}}+O({\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{3})\big)\,.

(240)

This property holds for general asymptotically constant functions
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
,
and reflects a general property of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
. This makes the delocalization correction to the identity operator in (
239
) safer even for slow-decaying field perturbations.

Similar properties hold for the inverse operator
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
, (
170
), whose kernel (
125
),

E^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\;=\;\delta^{\mathrm{d}{-}1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\,-\,\Big(\tfrac{W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{\big|}}\,+\,\tfrac{W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{\big|}\,}\Big)\,+\,2\tfrac{W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{\big|}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}{}\,\tfrac{W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{\big|}\,}\,,

(241)

can be derived from (
235
) following the steps of Lemma
A.1
implying the regularization. Again,

\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\defeq{\hskip 1.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral{I}\hskip 1.0pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt}+{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\,,

(242)

since in the nonlocal correction the leading asymptotic terms cancel
50
50

50

The sign at the first contribution in brackets is inverted by contributions from the latter nonlocal term.
:

\displaystyle\,E^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\,

\displaystyle\!\!=\!\!

\displaystyle\delta^{\mathrm{d}{-}1}({\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\,+\,\Big({\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{|}}}+{\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})}{\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W\vphantom{|}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\,}}\Big)\,+\,2\,{\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{|}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}{\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}W(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})}{\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W\vphantom{|}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{|}\,}}\,.\quad\;\;

(243)

All local factors in nonlocal correction are affected by the
average-free
projectors
{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}
, (
234
),
which ensure that nonlocal contributions decay faster than generic perturbations. For instance,

{\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{|}}}\defeq\tfrac{W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{\,{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{|}}-\tfrac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}}=\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})-\overline{{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)}{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{|}}

(and similarly for
W
).
The first term in the right hand side is of order
O(\tfrac{{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}})
, while the subtracted term is
O(\tfrac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}^{2}})
. However, the subtraction remove the leading potentially divergent integrated parts from the first term, so that when integrated with bounded asymptotically constant functions, they yield contributions quadratic in perturbations.
This reflects a general rule, that for bounded asymptotically constant functions,

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}{\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral\hskip-0.5pt\hskip-1.70717pt\widetilde{\hskip 1.70717pt\hskip 1.6pt{I}\hskip 0.3pt\hskip 1.70717pt}\hskip-1.70717pt\pdfliteral}\hskip 2.1pt}\mathcal{B}\;=\;{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{B}+{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}o(1)}\,,

(244)

showing improved convergence behavior of terms with average-free factors.
Accordingly, the kernel form (
243
), with average-free local factors, ensures improved decay of the integrated nonlocal correction:

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\mathcal{A}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\mathcal{B}\;\sim\;{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}O}{({\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{A}\,{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1})}+{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}O}{({\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\mathcal{B})}+{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}o(1)}\,,

(245)

in full analogy with the corresponding property (
240
) of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}\hskip 0.8pt
.

Finally, we verify the on-shell behavior of the effective cosmological constant
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
in the noncompact case. The action of
\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}
, (
241
), on a spatially-homogeneous function yields

{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}_{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime}\,}E^{-1}(t|{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}};{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}}^{\prime})\,\overline{f}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\>=\>\tfrac{W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{\overline{W^{-1}}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\vphantom{\big|}}\,\overline{f}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)\>=\>\tfrac{W^{-1}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t,{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{x}})}{(W^{-1})_{*}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)}\,\overline{f}(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}t)+{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}o(1)}\,,

(246)

where the contribution from the third term in (
241
) compensates the contribution from the identity operator, while the contribution from the fourth term inverts that from the second term.
In finite local expressions, spatial averages such as
\overline{W^{-1}}
can be replaced by their asymptotic values, since the difference
\overline{{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}W^{-1}}\sim{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}O}(\frac{{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}{\hskip 0.0pt\vartriangle}\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}V}})
vanishes in the limit
R{\,\to\,}\infty
. Using
\frac{1}{(W^{-1})_{*}}=W_{*}
, this simplifies further.
Thus, on-shell behavior of the effective cosmological constant takes the form

\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda\>\eomeq\>\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}F^{-1}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\mathbb{\pdfliteral E\pdfliteral}^{\,-1\!}\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\>=\>\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}F^{-1}\tfrac{W^{-1}}{\,\overline{W^{-1}\!}\vphantom{{|}}\,}\,\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\>=\>\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}^{-1}F^{-1}\tfrac{W_{*}}{W}\,\mathrm{c}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.1,0.15}\mathbf{0}}}\,,

(247)

(cf. (
136
)).
This confirms that the nonlocal behavior of
\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\varLambda
, which feels the asymptotic behavior of the perfect-fluid equation-of-state parameter
W({\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{\!$\sqrt{\gamma\hskip 0.5pt}\hskip 0.5pt\vphantom{|}$}}})
, survives in the noncompact case.

.

To obtain the Poisson brackets for the first-class constraint
\mathcal{P}_{\scriptscriptstyle{\!\!(I)}}
defined in (
48
), use the substitution
{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}f(\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!})\to{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}(f\color[rgb]{0,0,0.25}\pi_{\scriptscriptstyle{0}}{+}\overline{f}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!})
, which implies
f\to\overline{f}
and
f_{,{n}}\to 0
. Also note that:
\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}{\mathchoice{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}\textstyle{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}{{\mathop{\color[rgb]{0,0.1563,0}{\int}}}}}\,\overline{f}(\partial\eta)\,{F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}}=O\big(({\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}F_{\!{\scriptscriptstyle{(\!\mathrm{\hskip 0.5ptw}\!)}}}{H}_{{}^{{}_{\!\perp}}\!}})_{,{n}}\big){\,\weq\,}0
.
